- New finding: UC8179 and UC8279 use separate X4 drivers. UC8279 AA LUT selection depends on LUT_VER; variant 0x67 has no external grayscale mode, while other variants select QY/ZHX banks. Existing OneDrive system.log records only generic “UltraChip UC81xx variant detected”; no probe VER bytes/LUT_VER. SystemStatus exposes controller name but not LUT_VER. Exact source blob for XteinkDetect is identical to the v2.40-pinned SDK (`9d6f5398c786a43957826c10f75e557d00364a0b`), so there is no evidence of an X4Libris-only change in controller detection.
- Implication: current log cannot distinguish panel/LUT variants; matched physical font/ghost A/B needs controller variant in addition to textDarkness, AA override and panel starting state. Existing Lighter tone map remains a candidate for no added refresh; TTF threshold tuning may recover weight without changing glyph geometry, but hardware ghost outcome is unverified.

- 2026-10-27 literature/code follow-up: read Shen et al. 2018 (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6187556/) and He et al. 2020 (https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7281290/). These panel experiments attribute residual contours/ghosting to waveform, gray transitions and neighboring-pixel field/particle behavior; optimized waveforms improved ghosting and response but were validated on different panels. This makes Normal-vs-Lighter edge-state contrast a plausible hypothesis, not a verified X4 cause. Exact X4Libris path shows Lighter collapses raw1/raw2 to MSB but still runs the same gray refresh; `renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved()` calls LSB callback, MSB callback and `displayGrayBuffer()`. Skipping empty LSB text work could save CPU only and cannot remove gray-wave latency/ghosting; images require independent handling. No code/build/device changes. Next exact step: inspect UC8279 grayscale LUT and update path for documented safe per-tone controls; no LUT modifications.

- 2026-10-27 source follow-up: distinguished sparse text AA from image-dominant grayscale. `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp` uses variant-specific `kXtfAa02`/`kXtfAa68` stock AA tables for sparse text; the time-scaled X3 `kQualityBank` is chosen only when gray coverage exceeds default 25% or a Direct/Absolute/factory path is used. Ordinary text-only glyph coverage is documented ~1–2%; therefore the synthetic quality LUT is not implicated for ordinary text pages. Normal and Lighter still use the same AA LUT/displayGray sequence; only target planes differ. The 184 TTF threshold candidate changes edge/core classification only. No edits/builds/device work. Latest source evidence appended to report.

- 2026-10-27 upstream X4 ghosting source follow-up: official Witch(hunt) Reader 2.26 release notes report heavy ghosting after AA caused by charge pump remaining powered between AA and next B/W page; reduced-strength B/W could not clear grey charge: https://github.com/jpirnay/witchhunt-reader/releases/tag/2.26 . In preserved X4Libris snapshot, `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp` has post-AA `_redriveAfterGray` → `transitionGrayscaleBase()` → `runGrayscalePrecondition()` (`kXtfPreBwMid`) before next B/W baseline. This appears to address the same class but may add next-page time, and snapshot blob `6ede6e...` differs from current upstream Witchhunt SDK blob `1e8d77...`; cannot assume exact parity or universal success. Text AA/LUT threshold tuning alone cannot cure a failing post-AA transition. Next exact step: obtain upstream SDK history/code around 2.26 fix, compare sequence precisely to saved v3.1.2, evaluate if latency and visible ghost tradeoff can be measured without changing active user firmware. No local source change/build/device test.

- 2026-10-27 correction after exact upstream source verification: Witchhunt Reader 2.26 (release commit `d94f750b55c04cb07026180fdc934e79dfbba76c`) pins the official `Free-Ink/freeink-sdk` submodule at `11e7ad2e0ed81475dfe4a7c526c2fca3f2385714`; compared with 2.25 pin `ffeaaa271231d865590f8c54ea45ec02b1342d4e`. The exact 2.26 `Uc8279X4Driver::displayGray()` still sends `CMD_POWER_OFF` only when `turnOff` is true. So the earlier interpretation that the official fix was an unconditional POF was incorrect; the separate commit `df4a1b7...` has a message claiming that, but its actual diff also retains conditional POF. Correct mechanism in 2.26 source: after AA it sets `_redriveAfterGray`; the next Fast B/W display routes through `transitionGrayscaleBase()` and `runGrayscalePrecondition()` using stock `kXtfPreBwMid`, then restores the B/W RAM baseline. The 2.26 release notes' stated root cause is still relevant, but their shorthand must not be conflated with literal power-off implementation. The preserved X4Libris 3.1.2 source has the same general post-AA transition architecture, with implementation differences in plane restoration/direct-gray handling; exact physical equivalence remains unverified. This path replaces the normal next Fast refresh with a different precondition waveform/DRF, not necessarily an additional DRF; actual latency and ghost result require panel measurement. Official evidence: https://github.com/jpirnay/witchhunt-reader/releases/tag/2.26 ; pinned SDK source https://github.com/Free-Ink/freeink-sdk/blob/11e7ad2e0ed81475dfe4a7c526c2fca3f2385714/libs/display/FreeInkDisplay/src/driver/Uc8279X4Driver.cpp ; SDK commit metadata/diff https://github.com/Free-Ink/freeink-sdk/commit/df4a1b7b7be9f8ac75078b9fe6a2ea4e15639039 . No firmware edits/builds/device changes. Next: map local 3.1.2 vs exact 2.26 transitions step-by-step (conditions, LUT, CDI/TSSET, PTIN/PTL, PON/POF, DRF, base planes) and identify what can be read from logs vs requires a spare panel.

- 2026-10-27 follow-up: exact path comparison finds that local ordinary-text AA keeps `_isScreenOn=true` in `displayGray()` (reader calls `displayGrayBuffer()` with default `turnOffScreen=false`). The next normal Fast page goes through `transitionGrayscaleBase()` and `runGrayscalePrecondition()`; sequence uploads new BW to DTM2, runs full-window PTIN/PTL with stock `kXtfPreBwMid`, CDI `0xD7`, TSSET fast, PON only if driver state says off, DRF, PTOUT, then uploads BW into DTM1. Thus the local code also does not literally force a pump power-cycle after reading AA. It relies on the pre-BW transition waveform. Local `displayGrayscaleBase()` further insists on Fast fallback to take this path; Full/Half fallbacks go through normal BW refresh. Direct-gray pages instead set `_needFullClear` and disable the redrive route.
- Coverage gap found read-only: the existing X4 host case in `test_pro.cpp::testUc8279X4WaveformSelection()` checks AA LUT selection/tone classes but not the subsequent `display(Fast)` after AA or emitted precondition sequence. `rg` finds no test of `transitionGrayscaleBase`/`runGrayscalePrecondition`; the separate `test_uc8279.cpp` targets the generic UC8279/X3 driver. This leaves a key ghost-cleanup state transition without visible host assertions in this snapshot; it does not prove a defect. Do not add/run tests while user is testing.
- Font study implication: main suspect remains AA-to-BW charge handling, with TTF threshold changes only secondary. No source/build/device changes. Next: wait for spare X4/device evidence; prepare a manually reviewable event/command checklist for a future physical A/B (AA page -> next Fast BW; AA page -> Half/Full; Normal/Lighter; same font, controller LUT_VER, fixed page); continue no-touch study meanwhile.

- 2026-10-27 font-path follow-up: verified exact FreeInkFont flags/options. `TtfEpdFont::initFace()` selects `HintingMode::Default`. The saved PlatformIO build enables CFF and PSNames but does not enable `FREEINK_FONT_ENABLE_AUTOHINT`, `FREEINK_FONT_ENABLE_NATIVE_HINTING`, or MONO; FreeInkFont documents these as opt-in modules, with AutoHint required for `Auto`, `Light`, and stem darkening. Thus the runtime Light-hinting idea is not available in the tested build as-is. FreeType's official API says LIGHT hinting snaps vertically, preserves horizontal spacing better, but often makes glyphs fuzzier; it remains grayscale and cannot reduce panel AA waveform activity. Current host comparison of `Auto` vs `Default` changed 1.88–7.17% of pixels on 480x800 pages and changed ink area in both directions by font/size, so it is not a uniform boldness or quality improvement. Recommendation for research: do not enable Auto/Light globally; candidate typography work should stay on cached per-glyph coverage mapping, which adds no new display pass and avoids outline/advance changes. Residual risk for that candidate remains physical panel ghost and visible weight. Evidence: local `TtfEpdFont.cpp`, `FtFont.cpp`, platformio config, FreeInkFont README; FreeType official glyph retrieval docs https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html . Host artifacts already present: `autohint-vs-default-raster-20261026.json`, `autohint-vs-default-16pt-visual-20261026.png`. No source/build/device changes. Next exact step: continue evaluating cached coverage-only variants against fixed Russian page glyph metrics, then keep physical A/B pending spare X4.

- 2026-10-27 wider Lighter threshold host sweep: added a reproducible script and JSON for 4 families (Bookerly, Noto Sans, Inter, Ubuntu) × 12/16/18/20 pt, using a Russian prose corpus and runtime-like 150-DPI sizing. Host FreeType 2.13.2, NORMAL target, NO_AUTOHINT/NO_BITMAP; source uses TtfEpdFont-compatible 64/128/192 quantization then models Lighter as coverage<64 white, 64..t-1 one light gray, >=t black. At t=184, single-gray samples fall 2.9–11.7% (mean 5.6%) and black area grows by 0.86–3.13 percentage points (mean 1.49 pp). At t=176, gray samples fall 6.6–22.2% (mean 12.2%) and black grows 1.92–5.47 pp (mean 3.28 pp). Since this remap is applied while a glyph is rasterized into the existing 2bpp cache, advances, glyph geometry, wraps, cache bytes and refresh calls remain unchanged; only the proportion of gray vs black target pixels changes. This may strengthen strokes while reducing gray-target edge charge in Lighter, but does not skip the AA refresh and cannot prove lower ghosting on glass; 176 risks visibly harsher/jaggier edges. Candidate order: 184 first; 176 only if measured too light. Artifacts: `study_lighter_threshold_coverage_host_20261027.py`, `lighter-threshold-coverage-host-20261027.json`. This is diagnostic host FreeType 2.13.2 per-glyph coverage, not the vendored 2.13.3 full-page renderer or device. No firmware edits/builds/tests/device. Next: when a spare panel exists, compare 192 vs 184 under Lighter with fixed page/font/LUT/state; keep 176 secondary.

Full 480×800 wrapped Russian page, Bookerly/Noto Sans/Inter/Ubuntu at 16 and 20 pt, thresholds 192/184/176. At 184, 0.75–3.20% of ink pixels (mean 1.52%) switch gray→black; at 176, 1.64–5.05% (mean 3.32%). Visual sheet: 184 near baseline; 176 visibly darker. Wrapping is identical by construction. Full page exact FreeType supplements—but is not directly comparable to—the previous per-glyph system FreeType 2.13.2 sweep. - 2026-10-27 AA-waveform/dither study: traced local source. `Maximum` darkness only suppresses writes into grayscale plane masks; reader AA stays enabled. X4 `renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved()` still calls `displayGrayBuffer()`; display facade forwards to driver, and UC8279 X4's `displayGray()` still selects LUTs and issues DRF even for empty AA planes. So Maximum alone is NOT grayscale-waveform-off. AA-off bypasses this AA tail but current BW mask renders all nonwhite glyph coverage solid black. Built exact-vendored-FreeType 2.13.3 host coverage raster and compared 4-level AA vs B/W cutoff and Bayer 4x4/8x8 (4 fonts x 16/20pt). Hard threshold mean darkness +1.75 pp vs illustrative AA; Bayer results within roughly ±0.43 pp. Enlarged detail shows visible ordered texture. Hypothesis: cache 1-bit dithered glyphs and use AA-off B/W path could preserve average weight while avoiding second gray update; not tested on panel and may add CPU/visible grain. CrossPoint tool fork README reports 1-bit mono worse on device and uses small-size ppem/stem calibration instead; maintainer claim only, not reproduced. References: local GfxRenderer.cpp, EpubReaderActivity.cpp, FreeInkDisplay.cpp, Uc8279X4Driver.cpp; fork https://github.com/iwalton3/cpfont-editor. Artifacts under outputs/font-rendering-study: bw-dither-font-page-comparison-20261027.png, bw-dither-font-detail-20261027.png, bw-dither-font-page-results-20261027.json, render_bw_dither_font_pages_vendor_20261027.py, vendor_font_coverage_page.cpp, CMakeLists-font-coverage.txt. Host build/render succeeded; no firmware/device modifications. Next: inspect ppem calibration, correlate AA/BW tail logs, await spare physical A/B.

- 2026-10-27 ppem/stem-calibration follow-up: inspected CrossPoint calibration implementation and stem analyzer source (links recorded in report). Their tool scans ±1.5 pt, scores stem consistency/gray fringe, asks for manual visual selection, and by default limits automatic calibration to ≤12 pt. That implementation is for prebuilt `.cpfont` + gap-fix pipeline, so not directly portable to X4Libris runtime TTF/OTF. Added repeatable host scan `outputs\font-rendering-study\measure_stem_quantization_20261027.py`; result `stem-calibration-host-20261027.json`. Python 3.15 host FreeType 2.13.2; no PlatformIO use. Stem gray fraction varies strongly by face/PPEM (e.g. Noto Sans 16pt 30.4% at nominal vs 1.1% at +2 PPEM; Bookerly 18pt 30.4% nominal vs 13.1% at +2). Near-zero gray is not automatically desirable and can look pixelated; scan is diagnostic only and deliberately has no recommended ppem. Existing full-page static comparison shows short-probe picks changed line counts in 10/16 face-size cases (e.g. Bookerly 18 14→13; Inter 16 16→15), confirming reflow risk. Conclusion: keep ppem tuning as manually reviewed potential small-size option only; no global 18/20 change and no ghost/latency claim. Report updated at `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`. Checks: host script ran successfully, output JSON produced; source review and report/journal update only. No firmware edits, build, test, commit, publication or device activity. Next exact step: continue tone-map research without changing glyph advance/layout; retain ppem candidates for later visual/device A/B only.

- 2026-10-27 text-tone source matrix: re-read exact saved GfxRenderer drawMaskFor2BitMode(). Normal maps raw AA bins 1/2 to plane codes 10/01; Dark to 10/11; Extra Dark to 11/11; Lighter to 10/10; Maximum's gray masks are empty but X4 reader still issues gray display transaction. These are bitplane codes, not measured optical levels. Since only Normal uses 01, that particular state/transition is a concrete suspect for the user's Normal-only ghost observation; unproven until physical A/B with same page/controller/LUT/predecessor and AA timing. - 2026-10-27 experimental-control refinement: inspected exact fixed-size X4 displayGray path. Normal vs Lighter is the cleanest UI A/B: raw-1 AA pixels map identically; raw-2 shifts DTM1-only→DTM2-only; both still set one gray-plane bit per affected pixel. The renderer/driver process and transfer both full planes, load the same LUT bank and issue one gray DRF regardless of mask contents, so code-level refresh/transfer count is identical. This makes the pair useful for testing plane/tone effects without extra refresh-time work (actual elapsed time should still be measured). Added to report. No build/test/device action. - 2026-10-27 follow-up: inspected merged CrossPoint PR #3478 via GitHub integration. The change enables absolute grayscale for supported image screens, sleep overlays, and UC8279 image pages; it consolidates panel capability checks and fixes image-plane handling. Its `renderCharScaled()` / `renderCharImpl()` explicitly force text to B/W while absolute gray planes are enabled. Conclusion: this is not a no-ghost grayscale-font rendering method; it targets images and intentionally excludes grayscale glyph rendering in the absolute pass. No X4Libris edits/build/tests/device actions. Updated report `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`. Next: continue studying text-specific coverage mapping and AA-to-BW LUT/plane transitions, pending physical spare-device measurements.

- 2026-10-27 follow-up: created host-only Russian-page raster comparison for 1-bit coverage cutoffs 25/40/50/63% vs 4-level AA, four bundled fonts × 16/20 pt. Black glyph pixel area drops from 25% by mean 7.89% at 40%, 13.47% at 50%, and 18.43% at 63%, with identical host line breaks/positions because cutoff is applied after rasterization. This suggests a weight-tuning route for no-AA text without gray planes, but thin Cyrillic detail and physical legibility remain unverified. Exact v3.1.2 source has a 1-bit cache cutoff at 50%; active reader uses 2-bit cache, and its no-AA mask is effectively 25%. FreeType host is 2.13.2 vs firmware 2.13.3; results illustrative only. - 2026-10-27 follow-up: reviewed official FreeType `FT_LOAD_TARGET_MONO` docs and compared native MONO grid-fitting with normal-raster 50% cutoff on 4 bundled TTF families × 16/20 pt. Six of eight cases retained identical wrapping and pixel-mask IoU 0.97–0.99; ink area differed only +0.28–0.63%, so special MONO hinting adds little for those fonts. Ubuntu 20 pt had IoU .66 and up to 4 px line advance change; Ubuntu 16 pt changed line wrapping (same line count) and had IoU .25. Matches upstream warning that many scalable fonts lack useful B/W hints and small sizes may look poor. Do not enable globally; use existing NORMAL 50% one-bit cache as the eventual hardware baseline if available. Host FreeType 2.13.2 only; physical legibility, ghost, latency not measured. Report/script/results/PNGs updated; no firmware change/build/test/device action. Reference: https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html. - 2026-10-27 follow-up: mapped `drawMaskFor2BitMode()` to UC8279 stock AA LUT bytes. Normal sends raw-2 coverage to the driver-commented dark-gray target (LUT 0x22/0x23 BW/WB); Light sends it to light gray and Dark to black. This matches the user's physical report that Normal ghosts more than Light/Dark as a plausible target-tone cause, not a proven cause. Variant-specific stock AA LUTs differ too: QY (LUT_VER 0x02/03) encodes 2 VSL frames for those slots; ZHX (0x68/69) encodes 3, with the active group totaling 6 vs 7 frames. That may influence tone/settling/ghosting, but exact optics are unknown; do not edit factory LUTs absent X4 calibration. Most useful future device evidence is exact LUT_VER plus controlled Normal/Light/Dark page/photo/timing comparison; current log's generic UC81xx line cannot identify bank. Updated report. - 2026-10-27 follow-up: traced an important LUT-selector interaction in the preserved X4 v3.1.2 source. `copyGrayscaleMsb()` popcounts the MSB plane and switches from stock variant XTF-AA to scaled four-tone quality LUT only above 25% panel coverage (>96,000 set pixels on 480×800); either branch still sends five LUTs and one DRF, but the waveform bank differs. `drawMaskFor2BitMode()` makes MSB coverage depend on text-darkness: Normal puts AA bin1 in MSB/bin2 in LSB, Lighter puts both bins in MSB, Dark puts bin1 in MSB and bin2 in both. Thus a dense gray-image page could cross the selector threshold under one mode and not another; ordinary text pages should generally be far below it, so this is a conditional hypothesis, not a root cause. Important correction: the earlier 184 coverage-threshold candidate keeps refresh-call count but could alter the bank if a page is near 25%; do not claim same bank or implement without per-page/device evidence. - 2026-10-27 source trace: optional whole-book page total can add a deferred redraw when background spine indexing changes the visible total/approximation. `stepBackgroundSectionBuild()` sets `statusRefreshDeferred_`; after the borrowed framebuffer returns, `endBackgroundBorrow()` calls `requestUpdate()`, scheduling a normal reader render. - 2026-10-27 follow-up: traced reader's Single-push AA gate. It requires `renderer.supportsGrayFrame()`. `PanelDriver` defaults false, only `LgfxEpdDriver` overrides true, and X4's `Uc8279X4Driver` has no override; FreeInkDisplay delegates directly. Thus X4 cannot use single-push AA in this build; its reader path is B/W update plus gray overlay. Single-push is not a safe generic speed/ghost fix because the API restricts it to LUTs that can land gray from any source state. Report updated; no code/build/tests/device changes. Next: keep focus on validating X4 post-AA-to-BW conditioning asynchronously, with same DRF count and measured BUSY/full-turn/optical residual, rather than trying to enable single-push.
- 2026-10-27 follow-up: traced a new performance candidate from source. X4 `renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved()` rasterizes LSB during the async B/W DU, then rasterizes MSB after BUSY, adding the second pass to the AA tail. Existing `GfxRenderer::beginGrayCapture()` hooks can build both masks from the same glyph coverage in one look-ahead render; reader already retains these only on `supportsGrayFrame()` panels. Candidate: for image-free pre-rendered pages only, keep both masks (2 × 48,000 B = 96 KB PSRAM) beside the B/W page; at turn send them through the same X4 overlay sequence after BW BUSY. Same glyph pixels/LUT/two DRFs, so no expected panel-image change; possible shorter AA tail, but ghost not improved by the same waveforms. X4 overlay stagingWhileBusy=false, therefore masks must stay in RAM until B/W finishes. Must invalidate/release on cache/key changes and fallback if allocation fails; extra idle capture writes/energy and pre-render behavior need measurement. Report updated. No code/build/tests/device changes. Next physical A/B on no-image pages: current two renders vs retained look-ahead capture; record BUSY, total turn/AA tail, free/largest PSRAM, idle energy, and invalidation/cancel/fallback correctness.
- 2026-10-27 candidate audit: `PreRenderedPage` currently stores only ready/spine/page/timing/incomplete; it does not own plane bytes or store font/settings identity. Current render invalidations usually discard the page, but retained masks need stronger identity (page, font/style/size, darkness, orientation/layout, AA LUT selection and image-free flag) and atomic ownership. The forward page-turn handoff intentionally clears ready while preserving the staged-plane marker until consumption; many other navigation/overlay/refresh/settings/failure paths drop page and marker separately. One dictionary-suspend path assigns `preRenderedPage={}` without resetting the old boolean; benign as a stale bool now, but unsafe if it becomes ownership. Proposal refined: centralize `discardPreRenderedPage()` for all invalidations; separate one-shot consume path; mismatch/OOM falls back to existing rendering. Report updated. No code/build/tests/device changes. Next: if eventually implemented in an isolated copy, first add cache-key/ownership invariant checks and audit every existing invalidation path before physical performance/ghost A/B.

- 2026-10-27 dither-pattern follow-up: compared Bayer with blue/green-noise literature. Ulichney's blue-noise halftoning pushes quantization texture to higher spatial frequencies; the blue/green review notes dispersed blue-noise relies on isolated-dot stability, while green-noise clusters can tolerate dot distortion at lower apparent resolution. These are not X4 experiments. - 2026-10-27 pipeline-fit audit for mono-dither text: current reader has a BW `Page::render` pass then separate grayscale callbacks for `Page::renderTextOnly()` and `Page::renderImagesFromGrayscaleCache()`. AA-on prewarms 1-bit Atkinson + grayscale Bayer image caches; AA-off uses monochrome images. Thus text dithering can be separated from images: text-only pages might use the standard BW DU with fixed-position binary glyph coverage and skip both gray planes; mixed pages must retain image gray planes, so no turn-speed gain expected, though text glyphs would avoid gray states. Global `aaEnabledForThisRender` currently couples text, image-cache prewarm, and whole AA decision; a future implementation needs separate text mode/image-gray gate, preserve image ghost-cleanup behavior, and index after logical→physical orientation transforms. 2-bit glyph raw levels 0..3 must use panel-measured densities, not nominal 1/3 and 2/3. Must measure binary pixel switching/DU residual, DRFs, BUSY/full turn and native-size text vs AA-off. Source paths/line anchors noted in report. No source/build/test/device change. Next: continue evaluating physical EPD dot stability and distinguish true text-only speed gain from mixed-page behavior.

## 2026-10-27 — assess binary hinting and dither against quality constraints
- Project/machine: X4Libris font rendering / MyO, Windows; saved snapshot `work\X4Libris-v3.1.2-statusbar-step-test-no-tilde-20261026`.
- New evidence: official FreeType docs say `FT_LOAD_TARGET_MONO` is for 1-bit output; proper mono outline hinting depends on TT mono instructions that many modern fonts lack; changing render target can require recomputation/cache changes. The saved firmware disables FreeType auto-hinter and native TT interpreter, so mono target alone is not a supported quality fix. In X4 rendering, page BW goes first; deferred text AA uses short UC8279 LUT selected by LUT_VER. The four-tone quality bank is selected for high coverage (>25%; image-dominant) rather than typical sparse text edges. Peer-reviewed EPD halftoning work on a different 1-bit tri-color panel finds additional current from frequent pixel reversals, so monochrome dither cannot be presumed free of drive/ghost cost.
- Sources: [FreeType API reference](https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html); [Applied Optics halftoning study](https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32225289/); saved `TtfEpdFont.cpp`, `ftoption.h`, `EpubReaderActivity.cpp`, `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp`.
- Decision: rank font-aware mono shaping (for selected fonts/sizes) as lower display-transition risk but unverified; calibrated existing AA coverage mapping as the path to retain smoothness but still waveform-sensitive; keep fixed mono dithering as research only due to pixel-switch/current and texture risk.
- Checks: read-only source trace and primary documentation/paper review; no code/build/test/device change.
- Artifact: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Next step: continue investigating low-cost outline raster strategies that preserve layout metrics and work with the current FreeType feature set; separately establish the actual device LUT_VER before comparing AA paths.

## 2026-10-27 — glyph-cache insertion point for bounded monochrome edge shaping
- Scope: X4Libris font quality without adding gray-panel passes or page-turn work.
- Source evidence: in saved `lib/EpdFont/TtfEpdFont.cpp`, `faultGlyph()` rasterizes/quantizes on glyph cache miss and stores packed pixels; glyph advance and bitmap bearings are separate fields, so a pixel-only operation can preserve layout metrics. Existing BW renderer inks any nonzero glyph coverage on the BW page path.
- New candidate: apply a small capped coverage/stem-preservation correction to cached glyph pixels only, leaving advance/bearings/layout unchanged; this adds work only on cache misses and no new panel tone/DRF. Compare to current B/W threshold and FreeType mono output, first on host at native size; focus 12–16 px Cyrillic/CJK and counters/hairlines. No benefit is assumed until visual and device A/B.

Combined with [FreeType's mono-target caveats](https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html), this supports avoiding a blind global FT_LOAD_TARGET_MONO switch.
- Risks: miss-time delay; font- and size-specific artifact risk; binary BW still has the panel's inherent waveform residue. No guarantee of zero ghost.
- Checks: read-only source study and documentation review; no edits/build/test/device operation.

## 2026-10-27 — trace Auto+Mono integration cost on X4 reader
- Source trace: raw TTF/OTF manager uses `TtfEpdFont::load(pointSize)` default `twoBit=true`; Mono FreeType yields expanded 0/255, packed by current quantizer as only raw 0/3. The BW pass inks these as black; AA gray masks use only raw 1/2, so Auto+Mono text would emit no gray glyph pixels. CPFont bitmaps remain unchanged.
- Crucial constraint: X4 reader's global `aaEnabledForThisRender` still runs the grayscale plane pass and `displayGray` even on text-only pages; it couples text AA and image gray. Thus simply using Auto+Mono doesn't eliminate the delay/gray waveform. To meet no-slowdown, a future change would need to detect known-bitonal raw-font pages with no grayscale images and skip AA overlay, while preserving image cache/refresh/cleanup for mixed pages.
- Runtime/memory costs: glyph raster only on cache miss; 2-bit glyph cache size unchanged; FreeInk expands mono packed bitmap into retained per-face 8-bit scratch (largest glyph), and optional modules increase flash. Measure all before hardware.
- Evidence: `RawTtfFontManager.cpp`, `TtfEpdFont.cpp`, `GfxRenderer.cpp`, `EpubReaderActivity.cpp`, `FtFont.cpp`; static only.
- Checks: no build/test/device/code changes. Report updated: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Next: continue studying whether the X4 reader can omit a no-op text AA overlay safely without impacting image grayscale and DU ghost cleanup; if pursued, use page-level mode/contents metadata rather than trying to infer empty gray planes after update.

## 2026-10-27 — FreeType mono-hinting and stem-darkening guidance
- Reviewed official FreeType docs. `FT_LOAD_TARGET_MONO` is strong 1-bit hinting and may render small glyphs poorly when font B/W instructions are absent; `FT_LOAD_MONOCHROME` alone does not select it. Switching targets can recompute outlines and requires cache invalidation.
- Stem darkening is intended to offset gamma-corrected grayscale thinning; FreeType warns it can look heavy/fuzzy without gamma correction. Since X4 quantizes coverage to E Ink levels, enabling it is not a safe default. This is a source-informed inference, not physical X4 measurement.
- Recommendation: Auto+Mono host comparison with stem darkening off, per font/size/script; gate on advances/layout and glyph shape. Physical panel A/B still required to assess ghost and speed.
- Sources: https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html ; https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/hinting/text-rendering-general.html ; https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-auto_hinter.html
- Checks: read-only research; no code/build/test/device change. Report: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Exact next step: continue comparing documented rendering approaches and existing saved host artifacts; do not touch the user's test firmware.

## 2026-10-27 — refresh policy and EPD ghost evidence
- Read the saved v3.1.2 statusbar-step source: reader frequency counts page turns; 0 means FAST each page, nonzero emits HALF when its page budget expires; same-page status-bar redraws are explicitly not charged. Settings screens gate a hygiene HALF after four FASTs. The source comment's timing (HALF 2186 ms vs FAST 435 ms) is X3 data, not X4 validation.
- Current Microchip guidance and EPD waveform studies confirm residual is driven by particle/waveform history; full/reset cycles can reduce ghost but cost delay/flicker/power. Published waveforms are panel-specific and cannot be transplanted to X4 without vendor-supported LUTs/physical validation.
- Font-path implication: no extra page update is the only way to ensure a font change adds no panel switching/latency. Keep the existing waveform and update count; consider raster-only B/W glyph treatment, but Auto+Mono still has shape/metric risk and text-AA bypass must preserve image gray. Do not default to dithering (more black/white edge transitions). This does not promise zero existing ghost.
- Sources: https://developerhelp.microchip.com/xwiki/bin/view/software-tools/mgs/dev-kits/epd-ug/fundamentals/ ; https://www.mdpi.com/2072-666X/15/9/1076 ; https://web.eng.fiu.edu/gaquan/Papers/ESWEEK24Papers/EMSOFT/EMSOFT_40_Hu
- Checks: read-only source inspection and literature review. No code/build/test/device changes. Report: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Exact next step: continue evaluating methods that change cached glyph pixels only while leaving glyph metrics and refresh cadence unchanged; seek X4-panel-specific refresh/LUT facts before any claim about eliminating residual.

## 2026-10-27 — software ghost compensation literature
- Reviewed Hu et al., “Ghostbuster” (IEEE TCAD 2024, DOI 10.1109/TCAD.2024.3446711): model-based software predicts ghost error from previous image and adjusts new source pixels without hardware changes. Related SID 2008 work applies previous-image-aware halftoning.
- Not directly portable to X4: no proof for X4 panel/controller/LUT; would require prior-frame state, optical transition calibration, per-pixel CPU/memory, and could unevenly alter glyph antialiasing. Research lead only, not a firmware recommendation.
- Distinction: cached glyph-only correction (same waveform/update count) improves glyph shape but cannot cancel prior-frame residue; image compensation might counter residual but adds work and needs panel measurements; full/half waveform cleanup costs latency.
- Sources: https://research.polyu.edu.hk/en/publications/ghostbuster-a-software-approach-for-reducing-ghosting-effect-on-e/ ; https://sid.onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1889/1.3069761
- No code/build/test/device operations. Report path unchanged: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Exact next step: continue narrowing candidates around pixel-only font raster improvements and identify whether X4 controller exposes usable prior-frame / grayscale response data; no firmware changes without user request.

## 2026-10-27 — measured EPD tone curve as a glyph coverage LUT
- TTF coverage 0..255 is quantized to levels using fixed 64/128/192 thresholds on glyph-cache miss; source says thresholds match `.cpfont` converter to preserve stroke-weight parity, not measured X4 reflectance.
- Peer-reviewed EPD calibration study reports nonlinear, temperature-sensitive reflectance and measures/compensates curves on practical panels (Kao et al., IEEE/OSA JDT 2010; DOI 10.1109/JDT.2010.2068035). Hence equal coverage intervals need not yield equal-looking X4 gray steps.
- Candidate: measure X4's available output tones for the exact panel/controller/LUT; use a compact lookup/threshold mapping only at glyph-cache miss; preserve metrics, refresh cadence and 2bpp cache. This adds no per-page work, but retains grayscale states, cannot guarantee zero ghost, and must be calibrated by temperature/controller. Do not apply generic gamma or another panel's curve.
- Sources: https://scholar.lib.ntnu.edu.tw/en/publications/integrating-photometric-calibration-with-adaptive-image-halftonin-2/ ; https://www.mdpi.com/2072-666X/15/9/1076
- Static source/literature only; no implementation/build/test/device action. Report: `outputs\font-rendering-study\runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md`.
- Exact next step: obtain optical tone/transition observations for the physical X4 panel before recommending LUT thresholds; meanwhile retain Auto+Mono as the binary alternative and evaluate host artifacts without changing firmware.

## 2026-10-27 — E Ink AA is constrained by panel refresh waveform
- CrossPoint maintainer's explanation in issue #233: their gray text AA runs a fast B/W pass that temporarily paints gray-target pixels black, then a custom gray LUT pass. Transient thick text is the expected intermediate state. For one refresh, their documented trade-off is no gray text or a full-screen flash. This describes CrossPoint's implementation; exact parity with our UC8279 path is not yet proved.
- Issue #3461 separately reports delayed flicker (0.5–1 s) with AA on CrossPoint 1.6.0 on X4 Pro. A user comment guesses deferred grayscale rendering; no maintainer confirmation, so the mechanism is unverified.
- Issue #215 covers extracted stock CN 2-bit LUTs / fast vs quality modes, mainly SSD1677/XTC. It says LUT swap alone is insufficient due to distinct plane encoding and update commands. A later user on X4 Pro UC8279 LUT_VER=02 says AA edges still appeared jagged and hypothesizes UC8279 LUT or tone mapping. This remains user observation, not verified root cause; do not port the SSD1677 LUT.
- Research implication: inspect stock UC8279 X4 Pro waveform and logical-to-physical gray mapping against our current driver before proposing coverage changes. Font coverage calibration may improve edge tone but cannot by itself remove waveform ghost/flicker. Desired quality/no-ghost/no-slowdown/no-flash may be constrained by the panel's supported waveform.
- Sources: https://github.com/crosspoint-reader/crosspoint-reader/issues/233 ; https://github.com/crosspoint-reader/crosspoint-reader/issues/215 ; https://github.com/crosspoint-reader/crosspoint-reader/issues/3461 ; https://github.com/crosspoint-reader/crosspoint-reader/issues/692
- Read-only source/research; no firmware changes/build/tests/device actions. Report: outputs/font-rendering-study/runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md.

## 2026-10-27 — X4 Pro-specific grayscale path reconciled against source
- In the pinned code, the one-pass gray-frame interface is currently implemented by LgfxEpdDriver; the reader comments name T5S3 as its current consumer. X4 Pro UC8279 uses asynchronous B/W refresh followed by grayscale plane upload/gray update. Plane rendering overlaps the B/W waveform, hiding CPU latency, but the glass still gets a second gray update. This is mechanism-class consistent with CrossPoint #233's transient/refresh trade-off; not necessarily identical encoding.
- UC8279 X4 selects small AA LUTs by LUT_VER: 0x02/03 QY bank; 0x68/69 ZHX; 0x67 has no supported external-LUT grayscale. The separate multi-tone bank is documented as time-scaled from X3 XTH4 and hardware-validated on X4C, while AA/preconditioning LUTs are described as recovered from stock X4 Pro firmware.
- Driver comments state periodic HALF cleanup is promoted to FULL on grayscale base because HALF cannot clear gray-edge residual; FAST remains fast with the absolute-AA cleanup logic. This makes waveform/tone mapping a plausible ghost/quality trade-off independent of font raster quality.
- Important limitation: this is source documentation, not physical X4 Pro optical validation. Need exact LUT_VER plus a same-panel waveform/mapping comparison before any recommendation; previous log evidence was insufficiently explicit about LUT_VER. Simulator images cannot prove ghost residual.
- Files examined: EpubReaderActivity.cpp, FreeInkDisplay.cpp, Uc8279X4Driver.cpp/.h. No edits, builds, tests or device actions.
- CrossPoint source evidence: #233 staged AA explanation; #215 legacy SSD1677 LUT discussion and later UC8279 X4 Pro tester note.
- Next: inspect exactly how X4Pro AA gray codes map to physical levels and how stock UC8279 LUTs differ by panel variant; keep X3/X4 SSD1677 evidence separate.

## 2026-10-27 — UC8279 X4 cleanup comment/implementation mismatch
- Header says AA grayscale base must promote periodic HALF to FULL because HALF cannot clear gray edges.
- Actual implementation calls display() with the original fallback for non-FAST modes; display() passes HALF unchanged to displayStart(), where HALF uses complemented-target full-window scrub and FULL uses white-seed GC. No automatic promotion is present in the inspected path.
- After normal AA the driver sets _redriveAfterGray; the next FAST B/W page uses stock-derived non-flashing pre-BW transition; HALF and FULL are distinct cleanup waveforms. This may be intentional; the inconsistency is a source-review item, not proof of hardware failure.
- Implication: compare header contract, actual intended waveform protocol, and measured exact-LUT_VER residual before edits. Forcing full flashes blindly would harm latency. No source/build/test/device changes.
- Report: outputs/font-rendering-study/runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md.

## 2026-10-27 — exact glyph shade classes in the UC8279 AA path
- Ordinary sparse text coverage stays on the short stock AA LUT selected by UC8279 LUT_VER; high gray coverage/image surfaces select the scaled four-tone image bank. So glyph AA is not using the generic image bank.
- Source mapping: raw TTF pixels 0/1/2/3 = white/light edge/dark edge/black. Normal assigns raw 1 and 2 to separate gray channels; default Dark keeps raw 1 gray but sends raw 2 to black; Lighter maps raw 1 and 2 to the same light gray. These settings change edge transitions/pixel geometry, but do not remove the gray pass (except Maximum) or reduce waveform count.
- This supports a hypothesis for the user's observation that lighter/darker weight choices change ghost: the panel is seeing different gray/black transition classes, not less AA computation. Not physically proven.
- Candidate research direction: retain 2bpp glyph cache and metrics, consider a calibrated coverage-class map rather than hinting/dithering; use exact LUT_VER and evaluate long-duration residual, glyph readability, and page latency. No guarantee of zero ghost.
- Evidence: GfxRenderer.cpp drawMaskFor2BitMode; Uc8279X4Driver.cpp plane folding, short AA LUT, quality-coverage threshold. No edits/build/tests/device actions.
- Next: obtain exact variant-specific gray tone/transition measurements from UC8279 X4 Pro data and reconcile the header/implementation cleanup discrepancy before making any recommendation.

- 2026-10-27 raster-quality follow-up: Kodak low-resolution text study found hinted AA quality depends jointly on pixel/type size, bit depth and gray-lightness metric; hinting can improve the underlying stroke/spacing before AA, while gray edges may hurt at larger visual sizes. Caveat: LCD simulation calibrated to reflective-like contrast, not actual EPD. Compared to pinned X4Libris: TTF requests Default and build disables both AutoHint and native TT interpreter, so hinting remains a potential raster-only research axis; it does not add panel waveform by itself, but may cost first-render CPU and alter metrics/coverage. Glider/Caster notes on gamma-aware dithering are architecture-specific; FPGA no-latency dithering is not transferable to X4 SPI/UC8279, and text dithering could add visible dot texture while leaving gray refresh intact. Refined research order: hold display sequence fixed; compare one supported hinting/raster option at a time, then tone-map existing supported classes; defer dither/LUT work pending physical measurements and exact LUT_VER. No source/build/test/device changes. Report appended at outputs/font-rendering-study/runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md. Next exact step: inspect source's FreeType option gate/cache key and determine whether any raster-only mode can be assessed from the saved host artifacts without changing firmware; then document a controlled physical A/B protocol for the spare X4.

Both support using prior-frame state to compensate expected visible ghost; Ghostbuster's five-stage pipeline averaged 126.3 ms on a GPU-equipped Kirin 9000 phone, so it is not portable as-is to ESP32. Image-wide halftoning risks dot texture on text. - 2026-10-27 pinned UC8279 state trace (read-only): `_grayBase` stores a clean B/W framebuffer snapshot, not optical gray history. After AA the driver restores clean B/W data to both controller RAM planes and arms `_redriveAfterGray`; next Fast B/W draw may run the non-flashing precondition/re-drive. Half seeds the old plane inverse to target (all pixels transition); Full seeds white. This indicates explicit stateful cleanup, not an accumulating font cache, and does not prove/disprove physical residue buildup. Ghost compensation cannot directly index a prior optical-gray state from this buffer. Critical next observation: correlate page-turn and AA cadence with `8279x4_gray`, `8279x4_gray_pre_DRF`, and `8279x4_BW_TARGET_DRF` log event counts/timings; do not add refreshes or change waveforms before isolating the behavior. Files read: pinned Uc8279X4Driver.cpp/.h, EpubReaderActivity.cpp, HalDisplay.cpp. Report appended; no source/build/test/device changes. Exact next: map reader AA call sites to these driver event tags and annotate the physical observation sheet.

- 2026-10-27 reader-to-driver cadence trace: with AA enabled and secondary buffer available, ordinary X4 renders B/W async, renders gray planes inline and flushes/waits the gray pass; navigation preemption and buffer/settings gates may skip it. Single-push `supportsGrayFrame()` is a different route, identified by source comments as T5S3-oriented. After AA the next Fast UC8279 page is intercepted by `_redriveAfterGray` and uses `transitionGrayscaleBase()`/precondition instead of ordinary Fast diff. Thus page tests must separately record B/W mode, Inline AA plane/gray/restore times, next `8279x4_gray_pre_DRF`, and following FAST/HALF/FULL selection. This is a candidate explanation for cross-page effects, not proof of causation or RAM accumulation. Files traced: EpubReaderActivity.cpp normal/pre-render paths, HalDisplay.cpp, Uc8279X4Driver.cpp/.h. No code/build/test/device actions. Report appended. Exact next: correlate these events with existing system.log samples; if none available, preserve the page-by-page observation table for the next physical log.

- 2026-10-27 LUT compatibility audit: issue #215's extracted 110-byte LUT uses SSD1677 register format (0x32/0x03/0x04/0x2C), came from CN standard-X4 firmware V3.1.9_CH_X4_0117 and is for XTC/standard X4. Pinned X4 Pro UC8279 uploads five 49-byte LUTs via 0x20–0x24; AA banks branch on variant 0x02/0x03 vs 0x68/0x69, and vendor XTH4 tables distinguish QY/ZHX. So the popular fast 2-bit LUT is not portable to X4 Pro. A later issue #215 report says UC8279 0x02 AA looked jagged vs non-Pro X4 even after a driver change; anecdotal only, but supports measured tone mapping. Local evidence: `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp`, `Uc8279X4VendorLuts.h`. No edits/build/tests/device operations. Report appended. Exact next step: build an offline study plan for hinted 1-bit glyph raster vs existing 2-bit AA using existing host artifacts, with comparison metrics tied to no extra gray waveform; then check if local FreeType can produce a trustworthy 1-bit sample without touching firmware.

A future test would need same font/layout metrics, invalidation safety, compile-size and render timing, Russian glyph coverage, plus physical residual observations; only on a separate candidate copy/device.
- No firmware edits/builds/tests/device changes. Report appended: outputs/font-rendering-study/runtime-hinting-relevance-2026-10-26.md.

- Follow-up source trace: the X4 Inline AA interleaved pass always calls displayGrayBuffer after plane copies; UC8279 displayGray always issues the gray DRF and restoration. Maximum darkness zeros glyph gray masks but images may still use planes. No safe plane-occupancy skip exists in the traced path. Any future skip must prove both planes empty across text, images, decorations, abort/pre-render and reset/cleanup paths; no setting/page.hasImages shortcut is sufficient. Report appended; no edits/build/tests/device operations.


- Refinement to gray-plane occupancy hypothesis: UC8279X4Driver::copyGrayscaleMsb already popcounts its plane for quality-LUT selection; copyGrayscaleLsb also traverses its data while folding the B/W base. A boolean for both mask planes could potentially be accumulated in those existing loops with per-byte checks and no extra full-plane scan. No zero-plane fast path exists today, and MSB coverage alone is not a complete two-plane emptiness test.
- If both overlay masks are empty, the B/W base update precedes the gray pass, so skipping gray DRF may save a waveform on truly binary pages. Still must prove DTM1/DTM2 contents, gray-base flags, grayscale-history flag, redrive/full-clear state, cleanup and next refresh behavior; isolate Overlay only and never bypass gray images/Direct/Absolute paths. This is a promising hypothesis, not implemented or physically validated; binary text may be harsher.
- Report appended. No code/build/test/device actions.
- Exact next step: statically walk the entire controller state machine for empty Overlay planes through the next FAST/HALF/FULL page and identify required invariants before proposing an opt-in physical trial.


- Completed static empty-Overlay audit. Base B/W update precedes plane uploads; LSB/MSB fold reconstructs clean base in _grayBase; normal displayGray then runs DRF, restores that base to BOTH DTM1/DTM2 and arms redrive. A no-DRF candidate would have to restore both RAM planes, preserve baseline flags, avoid arming gray redrive, clear temporary state and honor power-off/abort/next FAST/HALF/FULL. Existing cleanup only reseeds DTM1, so simply skipping displayGray would be unsafe. This is static inference; physical controller behavior untested.
- Empty masks chiefly correspond to Maximum text darkness (hard B/W edges), so a skipped gray waveform might be faster but does not preserve soft AA; normal AA pages have nonempty masks. This is not the requested full quality/no-ghost/no-slowdown solution.
- Report appended, no code/build/tests/device action. Exact next step: research whether X4 Pro's existing UC8279 AA tone banks can deliver the same visually preferred edge tones with less residual charge at the exact LUT_VER, using authoritative source/calibration evidence; do not edit LUTs without physical panel measurements.


- 2026-10-27 AA waveform selection research: pinned UC8279 X4 Pro selects short 49-byte XTF_AA bank by LUT_VER (02/03 vs 68/69), but switches to a four-tone XTH4-derived kQualityBank scaled to 60% when MSB grayscale occupancy crosses 25%. Source calls this bank hardware-validated on X4C, not specifically X4 Pro. Page composition may change the waveform; causality for user ghost is unknown.
- fastAntiAliasing setting is labeled X3-only but its visibility gate allows every controller except SSD1677, including UC8279. SDK setter only stores the bool; repository search finds no consumer other than getter, and UC8279 driver ignores it. Treat as no-effect on X4 Pro and do not use it as an AA-mode A/B variable until corrected.

- 2026-10-27: проверена история CDI UC8179 в pinned source. В upstream commit e52d480 автор описывает конкретную регрессию: для AA нельзя менять CDI с 0x29 на 0xA9 после первой страницы; это накапливало ghost при быстрых перелистываниях и давало smear при следующем частичном меню. В нашей pinned v3.1.2 `Uc8179Driver::displayGray()` всегда передаёт 0x29 на AA активации — этот конкретный баг здесь отсутствует. Отдельный restore 0xA9 после B/W `displayFinish()` не является first/later AA split. Commit title про reset phase вводит в заблуждение: фактический diff убирает reset LUT эксперимент и возвращает штатные LUT + постоянный CDI. Также upstream a7bb60b описывает AA→FAST B/W через XTF_PRE_BW_MID; в нашем UC8179 уже есть `_redriveAfterGray`/`transitionGrayscaleBase`, надо сверить вызовы последовательных страниц. Не доказывает причину текущего гостинга, не знаем контроллер физического устройства. Никаких правок/сборки/тестов/операций с устройством. Следующий шаг: статически сопоставить последовательность `displayFinish`→`displayGray` и `_redriveAfterGray` на последовательных страницах UC8179 и отдельно изучить UC8279 LUT-bank/refresh, сохраняя различие между AA→B/W остатком и гостингом между AA страницами.

- 2026-10-27: уточнил tone mapping в pinned `drawMaskFor2BitMode`: настройка Lighter сводит обе исходные AA-категории raw1/raw2 к одному светло-серому значению; Normal распределяет их иначе. В исходном комментарии оценка AA букв 1–2%, поэтому на обычной текстовой странице настройка Normal/Light сама по себе вряд ли переключает bank.
