Вывод: эффект grayscale AA зависит от пиксельного/углового размера текста и уровня серого; hinted AA может помочь одним размерам и не помочь/ухудшить другим. Ограничение: изображения оценивали на LCD с имитацией белого/чёрного, не на реальном EPD, поэтому таблицы нельзя переносить на X4 как готовые значения. Практический кандидат — отдельная size-bucket tone LUT/coverage A/B без смены waveform и числа проходов; риск промежуточных серых ghost-переходов требует физического измерения. Источник: https://www.imaging.org/common/uploaded%20files/pdfs/Papers/2006/ICIS-0-736/33857.pdf . Заметка: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт] . Код/прошивка не менялись; сборка, тесты и устройство не запускались. Следующий шаг: проверить точное число реально используемых X4 tone levels и их LUT transitions в сохранённых driver/profile sources и уточнить, может ли size-specific mapping избежать новых waveform costs.- 2026-10-27: narrowed ghosting hypothesis by tracing X4 Pro AA tone selection through GfxRenderer and panel LUT. In X4 `textDarkness=Normal` uses both calibrated AA gray targets; `Dark` routes the darker fringe to black; `Lighter` routes both fringe classes to light gray. The ED047TC2 fast LUT supports only exact dark/light canvas levels per temperature band (dark indices 10/6/6/7/5/5/5; light 12/12/11/12/12/11/8, 15–38°C); arbitrary tones may be undriven. This matches the user's Normal-vs-Light/Dark symptom and points to dark-AA-tone transition as a testable hypothesis, not proof (stroke mask also changes). Low-risk discrimination: same device/page/temp compare Normal/Dark/Light and record selected LUT band; next isolated copy may alter only class-2 mapping while preserving refresh mode/count and exact LUT targets. Detailed trace: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт] . No firmware edits/build/tests/device operation.- 2026-10-27: verified waveform temperature selection lifetime in source: TPS65185 temperature is sampled in `buildConfig()` on the first driver begin; `lilygoT5S3LgfxConfig()` keeps the resulting LUT/AA gray targets in a static const config until setup is rerun after deep sleep. Thus temperature drift during a long active session does not update the selected 3°C waveform band. The vendor ED047TC2 comments and EPD literature confirm temperature-dependent, nonlinear electro-optic response, but correlation with this user's Normal-only ghost remains unproven. Added a measurement-only discrimination plan and warned against live LUT switching before correlation. Source: https://www.tandfonline.com/doi/abs/10.1080/15980316.2011.650865 ; detailed note: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт] . No firmware/build/test/device changes.
- 2026-10-27: source-audited Witchhunt 2.26’s AA→B/W ghosting claim against its pinned Free-Ink SDK. Found a directly matching UC8279 X4 driver fix: AA leaves the analog pump in a weaker powered state; the next fast B/W update may not scrub residual gray. SDK commit `df4a1b7` explicitly forces power-off after AA; separate UC8179/UC8279 work routes the fast post-AA base transition through non-flashing `XTF_PRE_BW_MID`/redrive logic. However X4 Pro batches use SSD1677, UC8179, or UC8279; Witchhunt “X4” is not sufficient to identify the user’s hardware. X4 Pro reference says SSD1677 has an internal booster, no external PMIC. This strengthens the hypothesis that post-AA controller/power sequencing, rather than font glyph coverage alone, can create ghosting, but hardware-specific behavior remains unproven. Future test: identify panel controller, compare AA→fast-BW transition with unchanged font/page; record latency separately; do not add full refresh per page. Source memo: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. No firmware edits/build/tests/device operations.

- 2026-10-27: audited the saved v3.1.2 reader/display source AA route. X4 Pro drivers advertise async B/W but do not override `supportsGrayFrame()` (default false), while facade reports buffer-held async only on X3. Thus this source routes X4 Pro through B/W refresh plus a separate AA grayscale activation; plane rendering can overlap B/W BUSY, but the AA waveform remains an extra activation. UC8179/UC8279 ordinary AA handlers mark the following Fast B/W page for stock non-flashing `XTF_PRE_BW_MID` transition; this is already a targeted attempt to avoid the full flash on the next page. One-pass SSD absolute-gray SDK commit `a3db714` claims host sequencing/build checks but explicitly leaves physical validation pending. Important next audit: establish exact controller and binary SDK revision, then trace whether the current font path selects ordinary overlay vs direct/absolute path and whether the post-AA transition is consumed; keep image/direct-gray paths separate. Detailed source audit: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. Source-only; no edits/build/tests/device operations.

- 2026-10-27: inspected available device log and reader vs BMP/sleep grayscale APIs. `system.log` last modified 2026-10-26 04:32 UTC confirms only generic UltraChip UC81xx; no raw VER/LUT_VER; no bootdiag.bin is locally present. Source: reader deliberately uses differential AA masks, while absolute two-plane mode is currently wired to BMP viewer/sleep screens only; GfxRenderer docs say absolute flag affects drawBitmap and reader glyph/page passes stay differential. SSD1677 Absolute mode can skip a separate B/W base; UC8179/UC8279 distinguish Absolute vs Direct/Combined, and support is LUT-version-dependent. This opens a promising study: extend reader to complete absolute planes (including text core, AA edges, white background, statusbar/images) and compare one final waveform against current B/W+AA sequence; risks are RAM, render cost, panel/LUT coverage and physical image quality. Current device log cannot distinguish UC8179 from UC8279, so no chip-specific recommendation yet. Detailed note: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. No edits/build/tests/device operations.

- 2026-10-27: продолжил исследование AA/ghosting. По merged CrossPoint PR #3478 абсолютные серые плоскости пока включены для заставок, обложек, BMP и UC8279-страниц с изображениями; не объявлены заменой текстового AA. В комментариях/commit summaries прямо оставлены проверки реального тона и остаточного ghost на устройстве. Рассмотрел альтернативы из литературы: spatial dithering может избегать серого waveform-перехода, но теряет локальную чёткость и может зернить мелкий текст; компенсация Ghostbuster требует измеренной истории/модели панели; waveform оптимизация в статьях специфична к панели и не переносится на X4 без контроллера/LUT и физических измерений. Сформировал безопасный будущий A/B: фиксировать геометрию/метрики/разбивку страниц/частоту обновления, сравнивать текущий gray AA, калиброванное coverage→gray и edge dithering по одинаковым переходам, отдельно мерить остаток на следующей странице и задержку. Источники и выводы: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. Код/прошивка не менялись; сборка, тесты и устройство не использовались. Следующий шаг: изучить расчётный компромисс пространственного dithering на малых кеглях и определить, какие измерения панели нужны до любого предложения по X4.
- 2026-10-27: обнаружил наиболее подходящий практический вариант для AA без серого waveform: CrossPoint PR #2461 (открыт, не слит) переводит имеющуюся 2-bit coverage глифов в один BW проход 64×64 blue-noise маской, пропуская LSB/MSB gray AA. Автор измерил X4: текст 765→584 ms (~1.3×), обложка 3330→600 ms (~5.6×); заявлены физические проверки на X4, но это не X4 Pro и не независимая верификация. Локальный source audit подтвердил совместимость входных данных: глифы уже 2-битные, нынешний BW проход закрашивает оба серых класса перед отдельными gray-проходами; значит кандидат может оставить контуры/метрики/перелистывание прежними и менять только отображение edge coverage. Ограничения: blue-noise узор может зернить мелкие размеры; 4 KiB таблица во flash и координатные вычисления на edge pixels; быстрый BW сам по себе не гарантирует полного отсутствия обычного diff ghost; X4 Pro и варианты контроллеров требуют физической проверки. Сравнил с закрытыми неслятыми PR #2956 (жёсткий порог — меньше smoothing) и #2542 (периодический checkerboard — риск видимого регулярного узора). Подробный разбор и экспериментальный протокол: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. Прошивку/исходники не менял, сборку/тесты/устройство не запускал. Следующий шаг: изучить точный blue-noise threshold/тональную маску и проверить на локальных 2-bit шрифтах, какие мелкие кегли будут выглядеть зернисто до любой X4 Pro прошивки.
- 2026-10-27: сопоставил новый upstream Fast AA PR #2461 с уже существующими локальными host-макетами (`bw-edge-dither-*`, спектральные метрики). Текущие 480×800 превью Bookerly/Noto Sans/OpenDyslexic уже сравнивают 32×32 V&C noise с Bayer/порогами: явного визуального выигрыша blue-noise на малом тексте не видно, хотя периодическая спектральная компонента ниже; фикстура не симулирует отражение/волновую форму панели. Критичная разница архитектуры: локальный `renderCharImpl` захватывает grayscale masks inline в том же проходе, тогда как CrossPoint PR измерил 1.3× на X4 целого рендера; такой прирост нельзя экстраполировать на X4Libris. У нас вероятный выигрыш ограничен исключением последующего gray display waveform и post-gray redrive; CPU может возрасти из-за lookup на краевые пиксели и текущей byte-packed glyph blit оптимизации. Значит 64×64 Fast AA — наиболее осмысленный тест на снижение серого остатка, но не подтверждённый способ одновременно убрать ghost и сохранить/ускорить листание. Анализ добавлен в [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. Для ответа на цель пока остаётся следующий исследовательский шаг: понять точную стоимость обхода packed glyph mask при blue-noise lookup и сопоставить с длительностью gray waveform в доступном X4Libris логе; потом физическая верификация X4 Pro.
- 2026-10-27: уточнил оценку CPU-пути по текущему `renderCharImpl`: Normal AA выполняет BW glyph blit и до двух дополнительных blit-проходов по bitmap в MSB/LSB capture; обход страницы/лейаут общий, но bitmap глифа читается повторно. Blue-noise dither, встроенный в BW blit, потенциально убирает эти два прохода плюс gray panel activation, но добавляет lookup на частично покрытых пикселях. В Portrait код и так собирает маски попиксельно, в Landscape byte-aligned путь оптимизирован 8-пиксельными битовыми операциями, поэтому CPU эффект будет различаться по ориентации. Это структурный вывод по коду, не замер. Доступный `system.log` от 26.10.2026 07:32:54 не содержит стадий `Page render`/`gray_display` и не позволяет посчитать выигрыш/замедление; зафиксировал это как недостающую телеметрию в отчёте. Изменений прошивки нет. Следующий шаг: сверить, есть ли в сохранённых debug/release logs подходящий отдельный тайминг конкретно для данной ветки; если нет, сформировать измерительный план без изменения прошивки и ждать отдельного устройства X4 Pro для физического результата.
- 2026-10-27: свёл точное кодирование уровней CrossPoint PR #2461 с локальными TTF/2-bit данными X4Libris. В upstream `bmpVal=3-raw`: 0 — самый тёмный, 3 — белый; в X4Libris raw 0 — белый, 1 — светло-серый, 2 — тёмно-серый, 3 — чёрный. Поэтому при явном инвертировании точное правило PR оставляет raw 3/2 сплошным чёрным, для raw 1 применяет blue-noise порог <96 (примерно 37.5% точек), raw 0 пропускает. Это не полутоновое сохранение покрытия всех трёх классов и способно утолщить край букв; порог не откалиброван под отражение X4 Pro. Значения скорости PR (765→584 мс для одной страницы) относятся к другой архитектуре и X4, их нельзя переносить на X4Libris/X4 Pro. Добавил сверку и ограничения в `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4-fast-aa-blue-noise-candidate-2026-10-27.md`. Исходники/прошивку не менял, сборку/тесты/устройство не запускал. Следующий шаг: изучить монотонные доли пространственной маски для всех трёх edge-классов и ограничения заметности зерна на 12/16/18/20 px; физическую калибровку оставить до доступности тестового X4 Pro.

- 2026-10-27: изучил многотоновый ordered/blue-noise screening по первичному обзору Kodak/SID и статье Ulichney. Для трёх AA edge-классов лучше исследовать один ранговый массив с вложенными порогами, а не три независимые маски: точки для более тёмного уровня сохраняют точки более светлого и добавляют новые, значит mapping плотности монотонный; lookup+compare локальный, без error-diffusion/соседского состояния. Целевая доля чёрных точек `p(raw)` должна быть `p(0)=0 <= p(1) <= p(2) <= p(3)=1`, но p1/p2 надо получить отражательным измерением native light/dark tone панели, а не переносить из PR. Частота точек/размер тайла влияет на зерно малых глифов. Зафиксировал в отчёте ссылки: Kodak/SID https://www.imaging.org/common/uploaded%20files/pdfs/Papers/1999/RP-0-93/1786.pdf, Ulichney https://cv.ulichney.com/papers/1994-filter-design.pdf. Отдельно отметил по EPO патенту, что B/W target не гарантирует отсутствие ghost: волновая форма и история переходов остаются фактором: https://data.epo.org/publication-server/rest/v1.2/publication-dates/20140924/patents/EP2054755NWB1/document.pdf. Код/прошивку/сборку/тесты/устройство не трогал. Следующий шаг: изучить, как panel-specific grayscale reflectance и накопленный ghost включаются в измерительный план без использования чужих порогов/неподходящего контроллера.

- 2026-10-27: проследил точную последовательность X4 AA в выбранном v3.1.2 исходнике: BW render + capture двух серых плоскостей → `triggerDisplayAsync(pageRefreshMode)` → генерация LSB пока идёт BW waveform → ожидание остатка waveform → запись LSB/MSB → отдельный `displayGrayBuffer(false)` для серой коррекции → `cleanupGrayscaleWithPreviousBuffer()`. Следовательно, след после AA может зависеть от отдельной gray waveform/очистки baseline, а не только от растра глифов. B/W dither уберёт этот серый touch-up, но не гарантирует очистку residual от B/W partial waveform/истории панели. Код уже имеет `Inline AA: planes/gray/restore` тайминг, но в присланном ранее system.log эти стадии отсутствуют. В отчёт добавил контролируемый A/B протокол: сравнивать BW без AA, текущую 4-tone AA после завершения gray touch-up и будущий dither; каждый блок начинать с одинаковой полной очистки, показывать один и тот же следующий B/W target page и проверять, остался ли контур предыдущего текста. Собирать фото до/после коррекции, mode/frequency counter и planes/gray/restore. Раздельно чистый старт и многостраничный накопительный прогон. Это вывод по исходнику, физически не проверено; код/сборка/устройство не трогал. Следующий шаг: запросить serial log X4 Pro с уже существующим AA timing и фото common target, при этом зафиксировать controller VER/LUT_VER.

- 2026-10-27: сверил enum TEXT_DARKNESS и drawMask: Normal реально выводит оба промежуточных серых уровня (raw1→light gray, raw2→dark gray); Dark оставляет один серый уровень (raw1→light gray, raw2→black); Light тоже один уровень, но объединяет raw1/raw2 в light gray; Extra Dark/Maximum сводят края к чёрному/без gray. Сообщение пользователя «Normal даёт ghost, Light/Dark почти нет» согласуется с проблемой использования двух gray состояний/LUT, но не доказывает её без фото/лога, где зафиксированы setting и controller LUT. Код/прошивку/сборку/тесты/устройство не трогал.

- 2026-10-27: перепроверил user observation Normal-only ghost с текущим драйвером. Она согласуется с картой тонов: Normal использует обе промежуточные gray state; Light и Dark оставляют по одной, но разными путями. Однако Light/Dark не ускоряют X4 Pro сами по себе: reader всё равно формирует/копирует обе plane, запускает `displayGrayBuffer()` и cleanup; UC8179/UC8279X4 также ставят `_redriveAfterGray` для следующего FAST-перехода (у UC8279 только не для direct pass). Значит текущая “чистота” на Light/Dark не доказывает отсутствие дополнительной gray транзакции и объясняется вероятнее отсутствием одного уровня тона. Старый user log от 26.10 содержит только общий UC81xx, без значения textDarkness, `planes/gray/restore` или VER/LUT_VER, поэтому причинность не подтверждена. Обновил исходный ghosting study в worktree и журнал. Никаких прошивочных изменений. Новый приоритет исследования: изучить, как корректно очищать/передавать пустую плоскость и сохранить `_redriveAfterGray`, если X4 Pro AA ограничить одним серым тоном; не пропускать SPI plane blindly, т.к. в controller RAM могут сохраниться старые пиксели.

- 2026-10-27: прошёл глубже в SDK plane API: `copyGrayscaleLsbBuffers` и `copyGrayscaleMsbBuffers` передают в драйвер обе полные панели; UC8279 X4 строит plane0 = B/W base + LSB, plane1 — из MSB, а затем восстанавливает чистую B/W base из пары plane0/plane1 и после DRF записывает baseline в оба controller plane. Пустая AA плоскость всё равно задаёт состояние; пропуск полной передачи может оставить старые selector bits от предыдущей страницы и испортить baseline. Bundled API одно-плоскостного shortcut не показывает. Значит, Light/Dark объясняет тоновую разницу, но не предполагает ускорения; экономия времени потребовала бы безопасной driver-level операции очистки/заполнения плоскости с сохранением grayBase/oldPlane/redrive. Добавил подтверждение к controller findings. Исходники не менял, сборку/тесты/устройство не использовал.

- 2026-10-27: проверил цепочку probe → controller → AA LUT. X4 Pro detector кладёт VER byte2/LUT_VER в `BoardConfig::ACTIVE.displayControllerVariant`; UC8279 AA выбирает фиксированный QY bank при 0x02/03 либо ZHX при 0x68/69, неизвестное значение использует ZHX fallback, 0x67 — OTP-only/no external gray. UC8179 использует свои fixed `kGrayLuts/kDarkGrayLut`. При этом probe выводит точные `[XTDET]` строки с `LUT_VER` напрямую в USB/UART `Serial`, а SD logger пишет только общее “UltraChip UC81xx variant detected”. Имеющийся system.log не содержит raw serial, textDarkness или AA таймингов; system.previous.log на ожидаемых desktop/E: путях отсутствует, crash_report не содержит controller. Итак, variant можно определить без прошивки по стартовому serial capture, если пользователь подключит устройство к USB/терминалу; точная физическая причина ghost пока не идентифицирована. Обновил report/controller study. Следующий шаг — если появится serial capture, сопоставить LUT_VER с LUT bank и одним и тем же AA test page; сейчас продолжить исследование waveform behavior по контроллерным исходникам, не менять firmware.

- 2026-10-27: quantified UC8279 X4 transfer budget from source. Plane: 100 B/row × 600 addressed rows (120 offset + 480 visible) = 60,000 B; board SPI 10 MHz → theoretical 48 ms/plane. After AA, the next Fast B/W update writes DTM2 plus a redrive DTM1 (2 planes), AA uploads LSB/MSB (2), then driver restores both B/W planes (2): 6 × 60 KB ≈288 ms wire payload before EPD BUSY, CPU, and row/SPI overhead. Some raster work overlaps B/W BUSY; this is a lower-bound data budget, not wall time. `EpubReaderActivity` already records `planes/gray/restore` and should be used when device timing becomes available. Also corrected the candidate assessment for native Direct grayscale: X4 Pro reader does not select it (`supportsGrayFrame` is false for all X4 Pro drivers), sparse glyph masks are not a complete absolute page, and direct AA sets `_directGrayOnPanel` + `_needFullClear`; the next B/W call's `paintDestination` path performs a target refresh then falls through to the normal non-fast refresh. This may defer additional waveform cost/flash to the next page, so Direct is not a validated net-speed improvement. Added caveat to `direct-grayscale-reader-path-2026-10-27.md` and budget to `x4pro-reader-aa-waveform-path-audit-2026-10-27.md`/controller report. Research priority now: tone mapping within current AA planes while preserving waveform count; no code, build, tests or device changes.

- 2026-10-27: audited the previous `x4-darkness-tone-path-ghosting-hypothesis` and found a board-mixup: ED047TC2/LilyGo T5 gray target indices and `LgfxEpdDriver` were incorrectly described as X4 Pro data. Appended an explicit correction; those T5 tone levels are invalid for X4 Pro. Revalidated generic text-darkness mapping against current GfxRenderer and UC8279 driver. Host FreeType 2.13.3 sample counts show the raw-2 fringe class is 0.69–1.88% of a 480×800 page for Bookerly/Inter/Noto/OpenDyslexic at 16/20; existing Lighter maps these pixels to the light tone while preserving black/white counts and glyph footprint, Dark maps them to black, and both retain the existing X4 AA path/plane transfers/activation count in source. Потенциально ближе к реальному «гостингу», чем статический font gamma, но требует calibration именно X4 module/controller, может искажать glyph stems/counters/diacritics и full-frame обработка может замедлить. Normal retains two distinct AA gray classes; Dark/Lighter collapse classes to one tone while still using AA grayscale activation. The UC8279 driver state machine and darkness mapping are semantically present in saved Witchhunt 2.40 too, so this saved source does not identify a new X4Libris driver change as root cause. SID 2008 paper (Feng/Gormish) and related patent describe a measured transition-error LUT fed into image-sequence error diffusion; the patent's calibration uses camera-captured panels. This is the first literature candidate here that directly targets temporal/history ghosting without changing display hardware. On X4 it would need accurate panel/LUT/temperature/history measurements and adds per-page computation; static cache-on-miss monochrome glyph quantization remains the performance-first candidate but cannot promise to remove BW waveform ghost. - 2026-10-27: identified two cache-stage controls already supported in the saved vector-font stack: the TTF adapter maps 8-bit coverage to 2-bit masks on glyph-cache misses using fixed 64/128/192 cuts, and FreeInk FtFont has `embolden26_6` implemented by FT_Outline_Embolden (currently zero in TtfEpdFont). A measured coverage-to-tone LUT or slight embolden could therefore alter text appearance without extra work on each page flip; AA waveform timing/ghost behavior itself would remain unchanged. FreeType recommends coverage/gamma handling for nonlinear display response, but its sRGB examples must not be transplanted to reflective EPD; thresholds require X4 panel measurements. OpenType gasp is font-side raster preference, not panel/waveform knowledge. Source refs: local `TtfEpdFont.cpp:337-357`, `FtFont.h:129-141`, `FtFont.cpp:615-626`; https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/hinting/text-rendering-general.html ; https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/typography/opentype/otspec190/gasp . - 2026-10-27: continued review of EPD-specific coverage/tone options. Primary FreeType guidance supports separating outline coverage from nonlinear display tone response and mentions emboldening to counter perceived thin strokes; sRGB numbers are not valid for reflective EPD. Source audit established three font paths: runtime TTF/OTF `TtfEpdFont`, built-in generated bitmap resources (`fontconvert.py`), and SD-card bitmap fonts (`fontconvert_sdcard.py`). A universal LUT change must cover all paths or equivalent fonts will render differently. Cache-stage LUT has no new per-page pass, but does not shorten X4 AA waveform; one-gray mapping may reduce ghost per user observations but remains a physical hypothesis; embolden risks counters/punctuation. A real coverage LUT would still need all three ingestion routes. More significant: reader `aaEnabledForThisRender` only checks AA toggle + secondary buffer/health; it does not inspect text darkness. - 2026-10-27: traced the UC8279X4 state around a possible Maximum-mode bypass. Normal B/W `displayFinish()` synchronizes DTM1 with the new framebuffer; `displayGray()` alone restores both planes and sets `_redriveAfterGray`; the next FAST page consumes that flag through `transitionGrayscaleBase`; new `displayStart()` clears transient gray bookkeeping. This supports (but does not prove) bypassing the redundant gray transaction when `TEXT_DARKNESS_MAXIMUM` and the page has no grayscale images. Images use independent masks and must not be skipped. Candidate physical sequence to validate: AA page → pure-text Maximum page → FAST page → Half/Full scrub. Main cost could be removed while output is intentionally binary; potential visual harshness remains. Appended source trace to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-tone-unification-and-aa-gate-2026-10-27.md`. Still no code/build/tests/device changes. Next: keep studying raster-side improvements that preserve smooth edges while staying on the faster B/W path; current literature supports calibration but provides no validated X4 thresholds.

- 2026-10-27: verified runtime TTF/OTF hinting state. `TtfEpdFont` requests Default; FreeInk's load flags use gray target + NO_AUTOHINT. In this snapshot the auto-hinter, 1-bit rasterizer and native TT bytecode interpreter are all opt-in and absent from platformio.ini; ftoption explicitly says without bytecode interpreter TTF glyphs load without native hinting. FreeType does parse `gasp` but no FreeInk call to `FT_Get_Gasp` exists; its flags are not used by this adapter. At 25-42 ppem, hinting could improve grid/stem consistency. It runs on cache misses, so warm page flips should be unaffected, though cold work and OTA size increase; auto-hinter plus mono raster is a candidate, native TTF interpreter carries stack-overflow risk called out by FreeInk and does not help CFF OTF. Do not enable blindly. Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-ttf-hinting-and-gasp-support-audit-2026-10-27.md`. Official sources: https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-gasp_table.html ; https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html . No code/build/tests/device changes. Next: compare the three future paths (unhinted grayscale, cached auto-hinted grayscale, cached auto-hinted mono) against no-AA waveform cost and per-format TTF/OTF behavior; find official/source-based footprint clues before hardware returns.

- 2026-10-27: source-audited `TtfEpdFont::load(twoBit=false)` against GfxRenderer. It would store glyph bitmaps at 1bpp (half the raw pixel arena versus 2bpp), but the generic 1-bit glyph branch writes every on-pixel to any active render mode, including both AA planes. Only 2-bit glyphs consult `drawMaskFor2BitMode` and can be suppressed by Maximum. Therefore one-bit raster alone does not skip page-level AA waveform; it needs reader/renderer coordination and an all-content AA eligibility decision, especially for mixed fonts and gray images. This corrects the oversimplified notion that mono raster automatically means fast/no-gray refresh. Addendum to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-ttf-hinting-and-gasp-support-audit-2026-10-27.md`. No source/build/test/device changes. Next: map page content/font IDs to determine whether a safe pure-text AA-skip predicate can be computed without an extra full page render; otherwise prioritize raster-level shape tuning and treat waveform timing separately.

- 2026-10-27: completed a page-level grayscale eligibility audit. The current reader AA gate is chosen before page content/font-format inspection; `Page` contains text lines, table text, standalone/table images, and `TextBlock` may switch to heading font IDs with per-word style faces. Actual glyph `is2Bit` is resolved at draw time. Two-bit glyphs consult tone masks; one-bit glyphs are written into all active planes. A safe skip would need to suppress 1-bit gray-plane writes, account for all actual glyph faces and image/table gray output, reuse classification from existing prewarm/cache work, and retain current behavior on uncertainty/abort/degraded buffers. No low-risk one-flag predicate found. Memo: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-page-grayscale-eligibility-2026-10-27.md`. No source/build/test/device changes. Next: continue source/literature study on ways to reduce ghost while preserving the current page rendering transaction; defer content-aware AA gating until a separate X4 permits physical validation.

- 2026-10-27: reviewed primary EPD software ghost-compensation research against X4. SID 2008 uses previous-image-aware digital halftoning for grayscale imagery; Ghostbuster (IEEE TCAD/EMSOFT 2024) predicts ghost deviations and pre-corrects source frames. The latter reports 126.3 ms average preprocessing on Huawei Mate 40 Pro and hides delay by preparing the next frame in parallel with refresh through dual buffers. X4's display stack has a ~48 KB previous-frame buffer, but this does not establish spare compute/RAM or an overlap window. Text-edge-only correction is an inference and needs X4 old→new transition calibration; wrong correction could change stem weight. No vendor waveform changes recommended. Memo: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-software-ghost-compensation-and-text-quality-2026-10-27.md`. No implementation/build/test/device changes. Next: audit X4 render/refresh scheduling and buffer ownership to determine if background frame preparation exists; continue with panel-calibration methodology and text-specific limits.

- 2026-10-27: audited whether Ghostbuster's dual-buffer overlap maps to X4 and found it does not in the current stack. `FreeInkDisplay::triggerDisplay()` calls X4 driver `displayStart()` inline; async refresh/frame-retention contracts are X3-specific. X4's secondary framebuffer is a prior-frame baseline, not a queued future-render buffer. Thus the research paper's 126.3 ms pre-processing cannot currently be hidden under X4 refresh; direct use likely violates the no-slowdown requirement. Updated memo `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-software-ghost-compensation-and-text-quality-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/test/device changes. Next: keep the method as an offline model/calibration reference and investigate low-cost text-edge transition metrics/algorithms that could be benchmarked before any device change.

- 2026-10-27: completed a FreeType Light-hinting source audit. Official docs say Light hinting snaps vertically (preserves horizontal spacing but may look softer). X4 build includes CFF and its driver advertises native Light hints; TT native hinter is disabled and TTF would need autofit. FreeInk's capability check says Light unsupported whenever autofit is absent, without inspecting CFF's native driver; the setter still stores the mode. TtfEpdFont currently uses Default, and FtFont exposes linearHoriAdvance for layout. This gives a focused possible no-warm-turn-cost experiment for OTF/CFF, but no guarantee of reduced ghosting; TTF needs a separate larger-risk route. Memo `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-light-hinting-ttf-vs-cff-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/test/device changes. Next: study CFF Light's actual mapping/compatibility in this bundled FreeType version and font tone quantization interaction; then define a measurable offline/hardware comparison when a test X4 is available.

- 2026-10-27: completed exact CFF Light path inspection. Bundled CFF uses Adobe engine by default, advertises native Light support, and FreeType selects autofit for Light only when the driver lacks native support. In pshinter Light turns off stem-width adjustment while retaining vertical blue-zone alignment of horizontal features; gray rendering leaves stems unsnapped. TtfEpdFont's 8-bit coverage still becomes the usual 2-bit cache, so this changes partial-coverage locations but does not bypass the gray waveform. `setRenderOptions` stores Light before returning false, and TtfEpdFont ignores the result (presently requests Default). A future disposable OTF/CFF Light experiment needs no added auto-hinter module, but can soften glyphs and does not prove less physical ghosting. Updated `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-light-hinting-ttf-vs-cff-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/tests/device changes. Next: investigate whether TTF files with embedded native hints can use a safe grid-fitting route without the disabled interpreter; compare on-host glyph masks only if allowed without touching firmware/tests, then keep panel ghost/time as required physical measures.

- 2026-10-27: mapped TTF hinting alternatives to X4 stack evidence. Native TT bytecode is compiled out; FreeType warns about deep `TT_RunIns` stack frames. ActivityManager allocates 24,576 B, while source comments preserve prior render-task free-watermark of 424 B and QEMU CFF preview 176 B under an earlier 16 KB stack (historical notes, not remeasured now). This makes native TT bytecode a poor first candidate. Auto-hinter is a separate bounded per-face/per-glyph path; it avoids that specific interpreter call-chain but still adds OTA size, heap/work and cold glyph latency. Auto-hinter + Light is a plausible cache-miss-only TTF experiment, while Normal risks increasing stem weight. Memo `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-ttf-hinting-stack-budget-2026-10-27.md`. No source/build/test/device changes. Next: inspect auto-hinter allocation lifecycle and code footprint evidence from this exact build's source/toolchain records; keep all performance and ghost claims provisional until a separate X4 test unit.

- 2026-10-27: audited the experimental auto-hinter's per-face lifecycle in the saved v3.1.2 snapshot. Auto-hinter is currently disabled, so it cannot explain current ghosting. If enabled later, FreeType keeps per-face glyph-style data until FT_Done_Face; TtfEpdFont::clearCache() retains bounded glyph/cache capacity, while memory reclaim frees cache buffers and tears down non-regular faces but intentionally retains regular. This is plateau/lifetime retention, not growth per page. Report: outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-autohinter-face-lifetime-2026-10-27.md. No firmware/source/build/test/device changes. - 2026-10-27: estimated auto-hinter memory from the exact saved firmware map and builtin fonts. The current map has ft_autofit.c.o with .text/.data/.bss = 0, confirming it is not in current image. FreeType allocates 2 bytes per glyph per live auto-hinted face for glyph_styles: actual builtin faces range 2,524 B (Ubuntu 1,262 glyphs) to 9,006 B (Noto Sans 4,503 glyphs), excluding globals/metrics/workspace/allocator overhead. Four initialized faces imply a 10-36 KiB lower-bound range for these families; no CJK fixture is present. Report: outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-autohinter-face-memory-estimate-2026-10-27.md. Thus gray-edge appearance and residual cannot be generalized from host raster or even across all units without recording controller/variant. - 2026-10-27: traced the actual reader-to-driver AA call path to validate the tone hypothesis. The AA-enable gate does not depend on text-darkness; Maximum zeroes glyph gray-plane masks but the normal plane routine still calls displayGrayBuffer, so it is not a refresh-mode/time shortcut. Uc8279X4Driver selects LUT by controller variant, not text darkness. Normal uses two distinct edge-gray states; Dark and Lighter each collapse glyph edges to one intermediate gray (different weight), making the user's reduced ghost observation consistent with a shade-transition effect under a constant refresh path. Still only a hypothesis; physical residual vs perception cannot be distinguished without the panel. - 2026-10-27: quantified existing Normal/Dark/Lighter host render modes and audited their source cost. On Bookerly 16/20 and Noto Sans 16/20, nominal darkness values are symmetric around Normal: Dark +0.42..0.68 percentage points, Lighter -0.42..0.68. Normal uses two intermediate edge tones; both alternatives collapse to one edge-gray state but in opposite weight directions. Source keeps the same AA page path, full LSB+MSB plane transfers, and controller LUT/waveform for all three at fixed settings; no added pass or refresh delay is introduced by switching among them.   Caveat appended: UC8279X4 selects its four-gray quality bank when gray coverage crosses a 25% threshold; setting changes can theoretically shift bank selection on image-heavy pages near that boundary. Keep first A/B text-only or log the actual bank.

- 2026-10-27: reviewed the existing middle-coverage-threshold sweep as a compromise between Normal's two gray edge states and the one-state Dark/Lighter modes. Raising only t2 128->144 removes 14-25% of raw dark-edge pixels; 128->160 removes 36-53%; black core, glyph layout and lines remain fixed in host model. 176 removes 65-77%, risking overly sparse edges. TTF quantizes on cache miss; .cpfont/SD-font converters use matching 4-bit cuts, so consistent implementation must update all three representation paths. This could reduce one transition without changing refresh schedule, but not eliminate gray waveform or prove zero ghost. Memo: outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-middle-coverage-threshold-candidate-2026-10-27.md. No source/build/tests/device changes. - 2026-10-27: verified EPD photometric-calibration literature: electrophoretic reflectance response is nonlinear and temperature-sensitive, and calibration work estimates reflectance curves across ambient temperatures; standards for reflective e-paper use reflection measurements and ambient-light context. This confirms host raster previews cannot select final AA tone/threshold or prove ghost-free behavior. Added a fixed-condition, within-panel test protocol; phone photos are only comparative unless camera/illumination are locked, not absolute reflectance. Note: `outputs/font-rendering-study/epd-tone-calibration-and-x4-bench-protocol-2026-10-27.md`. No firmware edits/build/test/device action. - 2026-10-27: new direct e-reader lead: Amazon US9190025B1 describes lighter font variants on non-flashing updates to compensate apparent EPD dot-gain/stroke thickening, increasing compensation across consecutive updates, restore default on flash; temperature/panel-batch calibration are also discussed. This is patent disclosure, not proof of implementation/efficacy; applicability to X4's BW-base + gray overlay + redrive sequence is unverified, and claims require IP review before reproducing specific method. Crucial distinction: font-weight compensation might stabilize same-page stroke weight, but cannot erase old-position remnants on a different page. Kodak 2006 reflective-display text study also shows AA benefit varies with angular size, hinting and bit depth; it used simulated conditions, not X4. Memo: `outputs/font-rendering-study/epd-font-dot-gain-compensation-literature-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/device changes. - 2026-10-27: found 2024 IEEE “Ghostbuster” software method: model pixel ghost error from previous/current image history, then precompensate source pixels; evaluated on real EPD, with per-panel calibration caveats. Earlier SID paper similarly uses prior-image-aware halftoning for grayscale imagery. Read-only X4 source audit: reader keeps prior complete BW base in `frameBufferActive` but sends AA LSB/MSB as a separate gray overlay; BW baseline alone lacks the previous 4-level target/physical history needed by such a predictor. Software precompensation could avoid a new panel waveform, but adds per-pixel compute/state (RAM/latency unmeasured) and is not text- or X4-validated. Memo: `outputs/font-rendering-study/epd-history-aware-ghost-compensation-x4-fit-2026-10-27.md`. No edits/build/tests/device. - 2026-10-27: deeper performance review of Ghostbuster (2024): authors' pipeline costs mean 126.3 ms on Huawei Mate 40 Pro/Kirin 9000/Mali-G78 with OpenCL, close to their cited 120–150 ms EPD fast waveform; they propose pipelining next-frame processing during refresh. Thus it is not a no-slowdown copy candidate for ESP32 X4. Their calibrated Carta testbed exhibited 1-step history with four observed tone states through 8-transition tests, but authors warn other EPDs may need N-step state, making automata impractical. Current X4 renderer only retains previous BW base; a full 480x800 2bpp prior target would be 96,000 bytes (calculated, not measured allocation). A tiny calibrated one-step transition LUT could be studied only if physical X4 data confirms short memory and compute can overlap existing page rendering. Memo: `outputs/font-rendering-study/ghostbuster-runtime-and-x4-state-cost-2026-10-27.md`. No source/build/tests/device changes. The SDK's optical-response `g_grayRemap` is for image/absolute-gray rendering, while reader AA uses separate differential planes and controller sequencing; copying that curve to text is unvalidated and could alter waveform transitions. Literature confirms EPD residual depends on previous optical state, so static font coverage mapping cannot guarantee to erase page-history ghosting. - 2026-10-27: traced X4 reader's actual AA work budget. X4 SSD1677/UC8179/UC8279 inherit `supportsGrayFrame=false`; inline AA overlaps LSB-plane rendering with asynchronous B/W refresh but then does MSB work and a separate `displayGrayBuffer()` activation. Thus it reduces wasted wall time but AA is not a one-waveform or zero-tail-latency path on X4. Current `BW` maps any nonzero 2-bit font value to solid ink; direct TTF is not using a dedicated monochrome hint mode (`RenderOptions.monochrome=false`, Default hinting). New candidate: compare hint-adjusted bilevel text, which could improve stems without any intermediate gray panel states or AA activation. FreeType warns native Mono hinting needs embedded TT B/W instructions and often degrades small fonts otherwise; `Light`/Auto availability and module footprint need separate evaluation. - 2026-10-27: statically inspected bundled font instruction tables. Bookerly (all four TTF faces) and Ubuntu Regular/Bold contain `fpgm`/`prep` plus extensive per-glyph bytecode; their `gasp` metadata requests grid fitting at the X4's 25–42 ppem range (UI 12–20 pt at 150 dpi). - 2026-10-27: checked FreeType 2.13.3 VM allocations and X4 reader render-task budget. TT bytecode operand stack is FreeType-allocator heap sized to `maxp.maxStackElements + 32`; on 32-bit ESP this is ~1.7–3.0 KiB for bundled Bookerly faces and ~5.25 KiB for Ubuntu, excluding other face/VM allocations. This is separate from the local FreeInkFont warning about deep C call stacks; X4 render task is fixed 24 KiB with stack high-water instrumentation. Real headroom and first-cache latency remain unmeasured. Updated `outputs/font-rendering-study/bundled-font-hint-data-study-20261027.md` and inventory JSON. No build/device tests. Next: inspect actual glyph-cache eviction and font-switch lifecycle to estimate how often bytecode would run, then prepare measurement gates for a test-only build if/when hardware is available.
- 2026-10-27: traced runtime TTF cache lifecycle. Regular face initializes eagerly; other styles lazily. Glyph misses run FreeType metrics+rasterization, hits bypass FreeType. Cache flush occurs at 32 KiB or 768 glyphs (clears index/data but retains allocation); `clearCache` also drops entries, `releaseResidentCaches` frees cache and deinitializes non-regular faces. Thus bytecode cost is first-use/cache-miss and can recur after flush/clear, not each cached page draw; book-specific flush frequency is unknown. Added exact measurement gates (font load/first-page, miss/render time, flushes, stack high-water, heap/PSRAM, steady page-turn and physical residue) to `outputs/font-rendering-study/bundled-font-hint-data-study-20261027.md`. No source/build/tests/device modifications. Next: examine risk of selectively activating bytecode per FreeType face and the actual stack high-water logging path; retain no-ghost/no-slowdown acceptance criteria.
- 2026-10-27: resolved how selective bytecode support would behave. It is a compile-time module; with it present, existing Default flags use native hinter automatically (interpreter v40), while Native only selects the v35/v40 interpreter property and that property is global/re-pinned before each glyph. Current `TtfEpdFont` sets Default on every vector face. Inter/Noto bundled fonts have no TT programs, but user TTFs might; `None` is not a transparent TT-only opt-out because it sets `FT_LOAD_NO_HINTING`. TTF layout uses linear advance, reducing pagination sensitivity, but raster bounds/stems still require checking. Added runtime-selectivity caveat to `bundled-font-hint-data-study-20261027.md`. No source/build/test changes. Next: inspect bytecode size in firmware map/build history or linker composition to estimate OTA cost without rebuilding, then define generic user-TTF safety checks.

This confirms the optional modules are not linked as executable data but does not estimate the size cost of enabling either module or TT interpreter. - 2026-10-27: added an evidence-based X4 Pro measurement protocol to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-waveform-literature-transfer-2026-10-27.md`. ISO/TR 9241-313:2025 (official ISO record) covers validated reflection measurements for reflective displays and demonstrates EPDs; IEC 62977-2-2:2020 standardizes ambient optical-performance measurements. Protocol separates glyph stroke/readability from old-page residual, fixes lighting/geometry, records exact controller/LUT and font settings, and measures transition directions and time-dependent accumulation. Miyao et al. supports assessing readability separately but has no X4-specific threshold. No firmware edits/build/device operations. Next: continue source/literature research on whether 2-bit antialias edge masks can be evaluated without confusing coverage with reflectance; when spare hardware exists, run the recorded controlled A/B.
- 2026-10-27: new literature sweep found no validated “free” software font technique that removes history ghost while retaining grayscale and unchanged refresh cost. A 2020 experimental EPD waveform study reports lower ghost and 300 ms faster drive through particle-activation waveform redesign; a 2025 16-gray EPD study uses dynamic-programmed waveforms; a 2014 real-time gray drawing pipeline combines halftoning with EPD drive control. These are panel-specific and cannot be transplanted to X4. Temporal dithering for text is therefore a poor default candidate due to extra frames and uncalibrated history. Added source-backed conclusion to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-waveform-literature-transfer-2026-10-27.md`. No firmware edits/build/device operations. Next: continue examining cached raster-side options (TTF hinting and coverage mapping) while preserving the refresh route; no winning visual change can be claimed until a physical A/B.
- 2026-10-27: checked current official FreeType glyph-target docs against X4's compiled setup. `LIGHT` is grayscale vertical-only hinting and can preserve spacing, but FreeType warns glyphs may look fuzzier; target-mode changes can trigger recomputation/cache invalidation; MONO can be poor at small sizes without embedded TTF B/W hints. In this X4 snapshot autofit is absent and CFF has a separate native path, so Light cannot be assumed to work uniformly for TTF and OTF. Official refs: https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html ; https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/hinting/text-rendering-general.html . Stem-darkening/gamma guidance is for nonlinear emissive display response and warns of heavy/fuzzy glyphs without linear-light compensation; its thresholds do not transfer to EPD. Added an implementation-specific caveat to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-freetype-hinting-availability-correction-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/tests/device changes. Next: investigate cache semantics and whether a per-format cached hint mode can improve glyph geometry while keeping page turns unchanged, then specify physical specimen/calibration criteria.
- 2026-10-27: source-traced raw-TTF cache lifecycle. Per-page `PrewarmScope` clears compressed/SD slots but not the live `TtfEpdFont` face map; raw vector glyphs are looked up by codepoint and FreeType rasterizes only on misses. Therefore a raster-side transform is paid once per cached glyph during ordinary reuse, while 32 KiB/768-glyph face overflow or font reload can cause repeat misses. This supports “no extra work on cache hits” but leaves cold-page/overflow latency unverified. Recorded in `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-freetype-hinting-availability-correction-2026-10-27.md`. No code/build/test/device changes. Next: design a page/character corpus that measures cache-miss timing and visual legibility on a physical X4, especially Cyrillic punctuation/diacritics at 12–20 pt and cache-overflow behavior.
- 2026-10-27: traced the surrounding reader pre-render path. The scan pass records text without rasterizing raw TTF; the normal real render then faults/cache-fills glyphs. A next-page pre-render also performs a real render ahead of the key press, records `preRenderDurationMs`, and page turns use a recorded hit/miss fast path. Thus cold glyph mapping cost can often be paid while the current page is displayed, but may reduce idle slack or surface on skipped/incomplete pre-renders/cache flushes. Added an exact measurement gate for pre-render duration, idle slack, hit rate, cache overflow and visible fallback to `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-ttf-cache-prerender-cost-2026-10-27.md`. No firmware/build/device changes. Next: use future controlled build logs plus physical captures to compare visual edge/residual and pre-render/visible latency independently.

- 2026-10-27: consulted official FreeType references for e-paper-relevant raster options. Light hinting is for gray output and can improve edge contrast while preserving spacing; strong Mono hinting is suitable mainly for TTF with embedded B/W instructions, and unsupported/small fonts can look poor. FreeInkFont documents Native's deep stack risk. Glyph cache is codepoint-keyed per face and returns bitmap before rasterization on hits; raster mode is not part of the key, so a future runtime mode switch must build before cache warm-up or flush/rebuild that face's cache. Practical candidate: optional Light/Auto grayscale first (cache-miss only, preserves AA waveform), then only if physical data supports it; it cannot fix waveform ghosts. Native only for proven hint-bearing TTF faces, with stack-budget proof. Memo: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт]. ## X3 text smoothing switch investigation — 2026-10-27
- Chat/thread: current X4Libris development chat. Project/machine: X4Libris firmware, MyO (Windows).
- Source snapshot inspected: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт] (archive has no `.git` metadata; unchanged).
- Findings: there are two distinct controls. `textAntiAliasing` controls whether EPUB/TXT/MD render through grayscale planes. `fastAntiAliasing` is the X3 fast-vs-OEM LUT preference, default ON and saved under `fastAntiAliasingV2`. `FreeInkDisplay::setFastGrayscaleLut()` only stores the boolean; repository search finds no consumer of `_fastGrayscaleLut`/`getFastGrayscaleLut()` in the display driver, and `Uc8253X3Driver::displayGray()` selects its grayscale banks from fixed mode/config, not that preference. Therefore the X3 fast-AA switch currently has no effective LUT selection; default-on appearance is not proof the toggle applied. This is a concrete wiring defect, not evidence that the generic text-AA switch itself cannot turn off.
- Recommendation: keep actual grayscale AA as an optional capability for now; prototype a separate monochrome “Clear text” rendering mode using glyph hinting/coverage threshold tuning (black/white output, current fast page waveform) rather than repurposing the broken LUT toggle. Do not promise reduced ghosting until tested on physical X3. In a separate experiment copy, either wire the X3 LUT control to a verified supported LUT choice or remove the nonfunctional control from the UI; do not touch the immutable v3.1.2 source snapshot.
- Changes/checks: read-only source-path inspection and repository searches only; no firmware files changed, no build/tests run, no device verification.
- Artifacts/logs: none produced.
- Next step: make a named isolated copy from the confirmed intermediate snapshot, then implement and validate a monochrome enhanced-text prototype plus make the X3 LUT switch truthful (functional or hidden). Preserve the source snapshot.

## 2026-10-27 — source trace of Normal text darkness and UC8279 AA
- Read-only trace in the local v3.1.2 font-step source snapshot (directory is not a Git checkout; no branch/commit available). TTF coverage is quantized to four raw classes while glyph advances are stored independently. Text-darkness mappings leave shaping/metrics alone but change panel tone classes: Normal uniquely uses both separate gray tones; Dark collapses raw2 into black; Lighter maps raw1/raw2 into light gray; Extra Dark maps both to black but still uses gray refresh; Maximum skips gray refresh.
- This matches the user's report that Normal ghosts more while Dark/Lighter barely do: leading hypothesis is the extra dark-gray transition/class, not a proven font-outline or rasterizer fault. UC8279 source chooses vendor AA bank by detected ID (0x02/03 QY, 0x68/69 ZHX); sparse glyph AA and image-dominant synthetic X3-derived grayscale use different banks. The driver also restores BW planes and redrives after AA, so actual latency/charge must be measured on the same panel.
- No firmware changes/build/tests/device actions. Detailed evidence and bounded A/B plan: [локальный абсолютный путь скрыт].
- Exact next step: on an X4 Pro test unit, record controller/LUT ID and compare Normal/Dark/Lighter on the identical book/page/font/size/refresh cadence, measuring residue and turn latency; first test only the dark-gray class effect, without changing geometry or global LUTs. Until then, keep this as a source hypothesis and continue evidence review.

- Followed `FreeInkDisplay::displayGrayBuffer()` and the UC8279 driver calls: `transitionGrayscaleBase()` is the B/W base refresh itself and replaces ordinary FAST after AA; it is not a third refresh in addition to B/W+AA. HALF likewise uses its scrub as the B/W base, then AA.

## 2026-10-27 — primary manufacturer guidance on panel waveform and font tone
- Read Good Display's primary FAQ and FreeType's official text-rendering guide. Do not transplant desktop gamma/stem-darkening settings onto the reflective X4 panel. ## 2026-10-27 — one-activation AA audit: Direct is not safe as-is
- Audited the current reader and FreeInk SDK path. X4 Pro UC8179/UC8279 do not advertise `supportsGrayFrame()`; that capability is only implemented by `LgfxEpdDriver`. Therefore X4 Pro uses B/W plus inline gray AA activation; overlapping render work does not collapse the gray phase into the same panel activation.
- `Absolute` planes remain a separate-base mode on UltraChip, so it does not eliminate the B/W base refresh there (SSD1677 advertises a combined base).
- `Direct` is the only low-level one-frame candidate on supported UltraChip variants, but UC8279 marks `_needFullClear=true` and `_directGrayOnPanel=true`; the next B/W page first paints the destination and then cannot use FAST differential, falling through to a full refresh. UC8279 LUT variant `0x67` declares grayscale unsupported. Direct is therefore not a valid no-slowdown solution in the present implementation; do not enable unchanged.
- This strengthens the later device A/B priority: keep current activation schedule fixed and test a single-gray-tone edge map vs Normal, preserving glyph metrics/raster positions; quantify residue on the next plain B/W page. It is a hypothesis aligned with the user's observed Normal-vs-Light/Dark result, not a cure claim.
- Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-one-activation-aa-feasibility-2026-10-27.md`.
- No source changes, build, tests, or device operation.
- Exact next step: when isolated device/log is available, identify UC controller/LUT variant and measure the one-gray-tone mapping first. Only investigate a revised Direct state machine if this fails the ghost/latency objective and the selected LUT supports Direct safely.

## 2026-10-27 — 184 candidate source integration and spacing invariant
- Verified current font pipelines. Runtime TTF compares original 8-bit coverage at 64/128/192, so exact 184 is directly representable with same 2-bit cache size. Built-in and SD `.cpfont` generators first downsample coverage to a 4-bit nibble; exact 184 is lost there (nibble 11 represents 176–191). Using nibble>=11 would mean 176, a stronger and different change.
- Found `fontconvert.py::shipped_level()` is also used for integer-pixel horizontal recentering. Changing it together with output tone threshold could move some glyph origins and affect perceived spacing even though advance widths stay fixed. A future candidate must keep the existing 192-based center correction and alter only final output pixel classification; then compare bitmap bearings/positions.
- Runtime threshold change adds no cache bytes or page-turn pass; it runs only when a glyph is rasterized into cache. Making it a live setting would require explicit cache invalidation/rebuild. Embedded and SD cpfont resources must be regenerated from source TTF; old 2-bit .cpfont cannot recover the lost 184–191 coverage distinction.
- Correction: prior host-page wrapping/white-pixel results do not prove identical per-glyph placement after converter re-centering. Spacing/bearing parity is now a required gate before device testing.
- Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-threshold-184-font-path-integration-audit-2026-10-27.md`.
- No firmware edits, build, tests or device operation.
- Exact next step: if moving to an isolated source copy, implement an output-only 184 threshold while preserving old centering weights; run deterministic glyph-bearing and page-layout comparisons across built-in, SD cpfont and TTF at 12/16/18/20 before asking for a device A/B.

## 2026-10-27 — font ghosting compensation research
- Reviewed manufacturer guidance and primary research on display ghost compensation. Good Display confirms residue depends on EPD waveform/controller/temperature; full B/W refresh clears ghosting but adds flash/time. This generic advice is not an X4 Pro-specific policy and does not meet the no-flash/no-slowdown goal.
- Found peer-reviewed Ghostbuster approach: predict residual display error and pre-adjust source pixels in software. It avoids requiring an extra panel refresh in principle, but needs a device-specific transition model and may alter apparent glyph edges. No transfer claim to X4 Pro without controller/LUT and calibrated panel captures.
- Decision: retain the output-only single-tone glyph-edge calibration (184 candidate) as the lowest-risk font-quality hypothesis; no periodic full-refresh policy. Model-based compensation is an offline research avenue only after controlled X4 Pro captures are available.
- Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-ghost-compensation-research-2026-10-27.md`.
- Sources: https://www.good-display.com/news/238.html ; https://research.polyu.edu.hk/en/publications/ghostbuster-a-software-approach-for-reducing-ghosting-effect-on-e/ ; https://web.eng.fiu.edu/gaquan/Papers/ESWEEK24Papers/EMSOFT/EMSOFT_40_Hu
- No source changes, builds, tests, downloads, or device operations.

## 2026-10-27 — single-tone AA plane-transfer budget
- New source-derived performance candidate: Lighter maps both glyph edge classes to MSB (`0x06`) and leaves LSB mask zero. UC8179/UC8279 still upload both gray planes and restore both planes on every AA page; no exact-zero-plane optimization exists.
- UC8279 payload is 60,000 bytes per plane (600 gates × 100 bytes). At default 10 MHz SPI, skipping the empty LSB upload and redundant DTM1 restore could save up to 96 ms raw wire time per page (24 ms if the 40 MHz profile is enabled), with the same gray waveform and pixel map. This is theoretical payload time, not measured page-turn speed.
- Safe only if the *rendered* LSB mask is exactly empty and DTM1 is proven to equal the B/W base; do not gate on the Lighter setting alone because image content may populate that plane. Full/Half refresh, first refresh, direct/absolute gray, cancellation and controller state need explicit coverage before any patch.
- Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-single-tone-aa-transfer-budget-2026-10-27.md`.
- No source changes/build/tests/device action.
- Exact next step: once an isolated X4 Pro is available, confirm panel/controller and compare Lighter against current Normal, then measure actual plane-transfer/AA timing. If Lighter improves residue, prototype the exact-empty LSB fast path in a disposable copy and verify panel RAM invariants before considering release.

## 2026-10-27 — correction to single-tone AA transfer idea
- Corrected the earlier proposal: zero LSB *mask* is not an empty DTM1 *plane*. `copyGrayscaleLsb()` builds `base | lsb` and writes its inverse, so with zero mask it still sends `~base`; skipping it would corrupt AA plane content. Do not implement the earlier DTM1 skip.
- Safer source-level candidate: skip only `displayGray()`'s post-AA DTM2 B/W rewrite. DTM1 is load-bearing as the next FAST old plane and `cleanupGrayscaleBuffers()` re-seeds DTM1; all audited X4 Pro activation paths write a fresh DTM2 target before DRF. If safe across remaining callers, this removes one 60,000-byte upload per AA display (~48 ms ideal at 10 MHz; ~12 ms at 40 MHz), without changing the rendered planes or waveform. Estimate only, not measured.
- Reviewed source paths in `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp`, `FreeInkDisplay.cpp`, and EpubReaderActivity; did not modify source or run tests/builds.
- Report: `outputs/font-rendering-study/x4pro-aa-plane-transfer-correction-2026-10-27.md`.
- Exact next step: static-audit all grayscale callers/cancellation/sleep/controller recovery, then add bus-recorder checks in a disposable copy. Only test the physical panel after a separate X4 Pro is available; retain the current test firmware unchanged.

DTM1 is duplicate only if the cleanup B/W page exactly matches the AA base snapshot; keep that transfer until equality is proven. Sleep/BMP/XTC image callers have different cleanup ordering.

## 2026-10-27 — reader AA frame identity
- Confirmed the inline reader swap sequence: `displayStart()` snapshots its B/W framebuffer pointer; `displayAsyncImpl()` swaps so that allocation becomes `frameBufferActive`; `renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved()` reaches gray display then cleanup without another swap. Cleanup passes the same address used for the AA base. AA reconstructs the B/W base from the valid LSB/MSB masks and `displayGray()` writes it to DTM1 before cleanup.
- This makes both DTM1 and DTM2 post-AA transfers strong duplicate candidates specifically in the inline Epub reader path (~96 ms ideal payload at 10 MHz / ~24 ms at 40 MHz). Any optimization needs explicit driver tracking of baseline identity/validity plus bus-recorder coverage; retain generic cleanup fallback and leave image/sleep paths alone.

## 2026-10-27 — monochrome pseudo-AA candidate for X4 Pro
- New source-level candidate: render FreeType glyph coverage directly into the normal B/W framebuffer, mapping only partial-coverage edge pixels to black/white with a fixed spatial threshold mask; keep high-coverage glyph interiors solid. This bypasses the separate gray AA planes/LUT and retains font advances, bearings, kerning, and layout metrics unchanged. A lightweight EPD project uses the same concept with an 8x8 Bayer mask, an edge-only threshold, and a small ink-weight bias.
- Potential benefit for our target: no gray waveform/plane upload on text pages, so page turn can stay on the existing B/W path and avoid the gray refresh's added time/residue. This is a hypothesis, not proof of ghost-free physical behavior; fast B/W refresh itself can still leave residuals.
- Risk: Bayer's periodic pattern can show as dots/texture at small sizes. A fixed blue-noise rank mask may look less structured with comparable constant-time lookup, but is not yet validated for glyph legibility on X4 Pro. Dithering modifies edge pixels (perceived contour) even though spacing/line metrics remain unchanged.
- Decision: do not alter firmware while the active image is under user test and no separate test device is present. If authorized later, prototype edge-only B/W pseudo-AA in a disposable copy, compare solid threshold / Bayer / blue-noise mask against existing Normal and Light on matched text, then require device checks for visible pattern, perceived weight, ghost residue after successive turns, and page-turn latency.
- Source reference (implementation only; not X4 Pro validation): https://tangled.org/jmrp.io/kleidos/blob/802a26d37dc7dc706e3682dbc6cb2a19e15fc5fe/src/hal/display/text_renderer.cpp . General blue-noise background: https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.09629 .
- No source edits, builds, tests, binaries, downloads, or device operations.

## 2026-10-27 — X4 Pro inline AA refresh cost verified from caller sequence
- Static caller audit confirms EPUB reader on a panel that supports async refresh starts the B/W page refresh, renders LSB while that waveform runs, waits for B/W completion, renders MSB, then calls `displayGrayBuffer()` for grayscale AA and restores baseline. Thus AA compute is partly overlapped, but the gray panel refresh remains an additional post-B/W phase; it is not a single refresh. `gt.displayMs` and `gt.restoreMs` are already logged separately by reader diagnostics.
- A direct B/W glyph-coverage mapper could avoid the post-B/W `displayGrayBuffer()` AA phase while using the same B/W page refresh. This is the strongest code-level reason to study pseudo-AA for both speed and lower gray-residue risk. It does not establish zero residual from the B/W partial waveform.
- The current AA setting also requires a secondary framebuffer; if unavailable/degraded, text AA is skipped. This is another reason a glyph-coverage-to-B/W path may reduce memory dependency, but it must preserve image AA behavior separately and must not regress current page composition.
- No source change/build/test/device action. Exact next step remains offline design comparison of solid threshold, small ordered mask, and fixed blue-noise edge mask; only implement in a disposable copy after the separate device is available/authorized.

It still runs `renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved()` and `displayGrayBuffer()` with empty text-gray masks. Therefore Maximum renders a hard B/W glyph footprint, but does not isolate/remove the gray waveform, nor should it be assumed to improve page-turn time.
- Diagnostic interpretation: compare Normal vs Maximum with text-AA enabled to test whether actual gray edge pixels are the residue source while preserving the same AA refresh path; compare AA OFF to remove the gray path entirely. A Maximum-vs-Normal difference implicates tone levels in the planes; no difference but AA-OFF improvement implicates the AA refresh sequence or plane handling. This is diagnostic only; Maximum is not a speed fix.

## 2026-10-27 — Maximum mode is a concrete wasted grayscale update across readers
- Expanded the Maximum-mode audit beyond EPUB: TXT and MD reader callers also run `renderGrayscalePlanesSequential()` whenever `SETTINGS.textAntiAliasing` is true, irrespective of `textDarkness`. For text/image draw paths at Maximum, `drawMaskFor2BitMode()` returns zero and scaled-image rendering returns early; nevertheless the sequential renderer uploads the planes and calls `displayGrayBuffer()`.
- FreeInkDisplay::displayGrayBuffer() has no empty-plane detection; it hands off to `_driver->displayGray()`. Thus the current Maximum choice does not avoid a panel grayscale cycle in EPUB/TXT/MD. This is a concrete avoidable refresh when the caller can establish both output planes are empty.
- Future disposable-copy patch concept: when effective darkness is exactly persisted Maximum and AA would produce no nonzero pixels, skip gray-plane allocation/render/upload/refresh for that page; preserve B/W output and all page geometry. Confirm all glyph/image paths, capture paths, overrides and non-EPUB readers before patch. Keep Lighter (persisted value 4) out of the `>=3` check; it still emits a light-gray plane. This can provide a true fast B/W Maximum baseline and validate whether the grey panel cycle is the residue source, but it is not final smooth text.

## 2026-10-27 — coverage precision available for a monochrome font path
- Static audit of direct TTF flow: FreeType returns 8-bit glyph coverage and keeps metrics separately; `TtfEpdFont::faultGlyph()` quantizes that to four levels at 64/128/192 thresholds and stores a packed 2-bpp glyph by default. GfxRenderer's BW pass then treats any nonwhite raw level (1,2,3) as solid ink. So the 8-bit coverage needed for a smoother pseudo-AA is currently discarded before the normal renderer sees the cached glyph.
- `EpdGlyph.advanceX` is computed from FreeType's 26.6 advance independently of coverage and bitmap packing. Therefore a future output-only coverage/dither mapping can preserve glyph advances, kerning, bearings, and page layout if it leaves the metric fields untouched.
- Design implication: the best first prototype point for direct TTF is the `faultGlyph()` conversion, while full 8-bit alpha is still available; but a cached 1-bpp dither mask needs a stable phase policy because glyphs move around the page. A later draw-time dither over 8-bit coverage avoids phase ambiguity but requires retaining/rasterizing more coverage and may increase cache/RAM cost. Existing 2-bpp input can support only coarse 25/50/75 edge coverage and may produce visibly stepped/dotted edges.
- Built-in `.cpfont` and some bitmap fonts already enter GfxRenderer as 2-bpp, so the same mechanism needs a separate/coarse fallback; do not assume a TTF-only fix solves all fonts.
- No source edits/build/tests/device action. Source refs: `FtFont.cpp` (8-bit coverage), `TtfEpdFont.cpp` (threshold packing and separate advance), `GfxRenderer.cpp` (BW treats every non-white level as ink).

## 2026-10-27 — FreeType hinting modes are not a geometry-preserving AA switch
- Checked current FreeType 2 API reference and X4Libris implementation. FreeType documents that changing load/render targets usually changes both outlines and raster bitmaps; callers should keep one mode/cache policy. `FT_RENDER_MODE_LIGHT` itself is the normal 8-bit grayscale raster mode; its distinction is the light hinting target selected during glyph loading.
- Our `FtFont` maps Light to `FT_LOAD_TARGET_LIGHT`, but renders with `FT_RENDER_MODE_NORMAL` unless monochrome was explicitly requested. The bitmap coverage is then reduced by `TtfEpdFont` to 2-bit levels at 64/128/192. Thus Light is still grayscale AA and still feeds the current grayscale-page pipeline; it is not a way to retain exact glyph shape while avoiding the extra e-paper refresh. Changing hinting can alter outline fitting/bitmap shape, so it conflicts with the user's requirement to keep glyph forms/spacing unchanged unless separately verified.
- Best current candidate remains a coverage-to-monochrome edge mask at the existing raster/cache boundary: preserve `advanceX`, bearings, kerning and line layout; change only which partial-coverage pixels become black/white, and keep the regular FAST B/W refresh path. It may visibly grain thin curves and cannot guarantee zero residual ghosting without device A/B. Keep this as a prototype candidate, not a claimed solution.
- Official source: FreeType glyph retrieval reference, https://freetype.org/freetype2/docs/reference/ft2-glyph_retrieval.html (render-mode/target and cache notes). Local code: `freeink-sdk/libs/font/FreeInkFont/src/FtFont.cpp` (`loadFlagsFor`, `renderModeFor`, `rasterizeGlyph26_6`) and `lib/EpdFont/TtfEpdFont.cpp` (coverage quantization and separately assigned advance).
- No code edits, tests, build, or device action. Exact next step: keep studying tone-to-B/W edge masks and panel FAST waveform behavior; only prepare an experimental firmware copy once a separate X4 Pro test device is available.

## 2026-10-27 — X4 Pro text-AA waveform cost and ghost-residue handling
- Traced the active EPUB inline-AA path and UC8279 X4 driver. CPU renders the LSB plane while the initial B/W waveform is active, then renders MSB and calls `displayGrayBuffer()`, which starts a separate grayscale DRF. Thus glyph raster work is overlapped, but preserving gray edge pixels still incurs an additional panel waveform after B/W; SPI plane transfers/restore also remain.
- The X4 driver encodes AA planes as absolute selectors (so black/white are distinct), restores the clean B/W base to both controller RAM planes after AA, and marks the next FAST page to use a non-flashing grayscale-base transition. Comments explicitly state AA edge charge can remain and a periodic Half request must promote to Full to clear it. This is the current deliberate ghost-control strategy, but it costs transitions/refresh time and periodic flashes.
- Therefore the only promising route to both a single ordinary FAST page refresh and no extra gray-waveform residue is to stop sending mid-gray panel levels for reader text and approximate edge coverage spatially in B/W. This is a genuine appearance trade-off, not yet proven better; test must compare glyph legibility, edge grain, cumulative ghost, and page-turn latency on the target panel/LUT variant. Do not simply remove the post-AA transition/clean: it currently maintains controller/panel charge state.
- No edits/build/tests/device action. Source: `GfxRenderer.h::renderGrayscalePlanesInterleaved`, `EpubReaderActivity.cpp` inline AA call, `Uc8279X4Driver.cpp::displayGray` and `display`/`transitionGrayscaleBase`; exact panel variant remains necessary for any eventual A/B.

## 2026-10-27 — static blue-noise mask is the better first dither candidate than Bayer
- Reviewed primary digital-halftoning work by Ulichney (1988) and later blue-noise mask work. The relevant design principle is suppressing low-frequency periodic artifacts: blue-noise dispersed dots tend to look less like a visible repeating grid than Bayer ordered patterns. This is general halftoning evidence, not e-paper/X4 Pro validation.
- For a no-flash e-paper glyph edge candidate, use a fixed spatial threshold mask (never rotate/change the mask per refresh); threshold only partial FreeType coverage, preserve solid glyph core and all advance/bearing/kerning/layout values, then use the existing B/W FAST waveform. A small rank mask adds negligible per-pixel work and no per-refresh state, while avoiding temporal dithering that would require repeated physical updates. A larger mask reduces obvious tiling but must be weighed against cache/storage and mask lookup cost.
- Risks remain material: e-paper's visible dot pitch and sparse text edges can expose grain; mapping 8-bit coverage into spatial black/white occupancy changes apparent edge geometry/weight despite preserving layout metrics. User-facing device A/B is mandatory; source papers do not prove clean reading at X4 Pro size/contrast.
- Sources: Ulichney, “Dithering with Blue Noise” (1988), https://cv.ulichney.com/papers/1988-blue-noise.pdf; “Methods for Generating Blue-Noise Dither Matrices for Digital Halftoning,” https://www.imaging.org/common/uploaded%20files/pdfs/Papers/1999/RP-0-93/1786.pdf. No implementation or tests.
- Next: determine whether the existing monochrome FAST waveform and periodic ghost-clean mechanism remain valid when reader AA is rendered as static spatial edges, then prepare a purely disposable firmware copy only when the second test device is available.
